## Folder 4 — nextwork/ (38 images)

**Folder-level baseline (applies to all nextwork/ slides unless noted):**

`@nextwork` is an **AWS-education creator brand** — teaches cloud/AWS concepts through visually-rich carousel + Reel explainers. Distinctive brand identity:

- **Signature composition:** every slide is a **rounded-rectangle "card"** (radius ~40–48 px) sitting inside a very dark canvas background — the card has ~20–40 px inset all around, exposing a thin dark frame around the entire slide. Card corners rounded, subtle shadow. Creates a "polaroid / postcard sitting on black velvet" feel.
- **Card content is FULL-BLEED painterly artwork or dark solid.** Painterly = warm, atmospheric, oil-painting-style illustrated scenes (vineyards, oceans, mountains, cities, workshops) generated by AI-image models with a very specific painterly-cinematic style. Palette leans warm gold + amber + burnt-sienna + soft green.
- **Top-of-card AWS service-icon row:** a row of **~7 rounded-square icons** at the top of each card, showing the AWS services relevant to the slide's topic. Each icon ~85 × 85 px, radius ~18 px, borderless. Icons are the actual AWS product logos (EC2, S3, Lambda, RDS, CloudWatch, IAM, etc.) plus the `aws` wordmark tile as the leftmost anchor.
- **Big serif-or-sans display headline** near the bottom of the card, left-aligned, white ink. Reads as the slide's question or statement.
- **Brand pill bottom-left:** a small white rounded-pill containing a hexagon logo mark + `nextwork` wordmark. Consistent across all slides.
- **Circular white nav chevron on right edge** (mid-card vertical), same primitive as Anthropic/Yan Liu's chevrons.
- **Dot indicator bottom-centre** — small dots row inside the card frame, active dot filled white.
- **Colour palette:** warm painterly earth tones (gold `#C89860`, amber `#E4A85D`, burnt-sienna `#A85838`, olive `#5D6B3A`, dark chocolate `#2B1F14`) + Anthropic-adjacent cream + pure white text on dark painterly scenes. Very cinematic, "warmth of a Pixar sunset".

Individual entries below focus on deltas.

### nextwork/image.png
**Pattern class:** chapter-cover / educational-carousel-cover (opener slide).
**Overall vibe (1 line):** Cinematic painterly vineyard-at-sunset with an AWS icon strip at top + `"How does a VPC actually work?"` display headline + `nextwork` brand pill — a **premium AWS-education carousel cover**.

- **Canvas:** 1080×1350 (portrait 4:5). Full canvas is a very dark near-black `~#0D0906` (deeper than Anthropic's `BG_COSMIC_DARK`, warmer/browner tint). The card sits inset ~25 px from all edges.

- **Card container (y≈25 → 1325, x≈25 → 1055):**
    - Rounded rectangle, radius ~44 px.
    - **Full-bleed painterly artwork inside the card:** an AI-generated oil-painting-style vineyard scene at golden-hour. Rolling amber/gold hills with parallel vine rows converging on a central dirt path. A **lone figure in a wide-brimmed hat** walks down the central path toward the vanishing point. Warm sun-drenched sky top-right (soft creamy yellow), deep burnt-sienna shadows in foreground, olive-green trees at horizon. Very cinematic, dreamlike.
    - Composition intent: **the walking figure = "the AWS user" journeying into VPC concepts**. Reinforced by the vanishing-point perspective drawing the eye toward the horizon (metaphor for "learning journey").
    - No visible drop shadow between card and canvas — the card's rounded edges just sit on the dark bg.

- **Layout zones inside the card (top → bottom):**
    - **Zone A — AWS icon strip (y≈60 → 145, x≈60 → 630, ~570 × 85 px):** horizontal row of **7 rounded-square icons**, each ~85 × 85 px, radius ~18 px, borderless. Small ~10 px gaps between icons. Left-anchored (not full-width — takes ~53% of card width).
        - Icon 1: `aws` wordmark on white bg — the AWS wordmark tile, always leftmost as brand anchor.
        - Icon 2: dark-teal/slate square with a chip/CPU glyph (looks like a compute service — probably **EC2** icon rendered abstractly, or **Compute** category).
        - Icon 3: forest-green square with a white bucket glyph — **S3** (Simple Storage Service).
        - Icon 4: orange/amber square with a stylised `λ` (lambda) glyph — **AWS Lambda**.
        - Icon 5: blue square with a horizontal database-cylinders-and-arrows glyph — **RDS** (Relational Database Service) or **DynamoDB**.
        - Icon 6: magenta/dark-pink square with a magnifying-glass-over-chart glyph — **CloudWatch** or an analytics/monitoring service.
        - Icon 7: purple/violet square with a cloud-and-shield glyph — likely **IAM** (Identity & Access Management) or **CloudTrail** (security/compliance).
        - The 7 icons **preview the AWS services this VPC carousel will touch** — reader immediately understands scope.
    - **Zone B — Painterly artwork (y≈145 → 1080, full card width):** the vineyard scene fills most of the card. AWS icon strip sits over the top of the artwork (with the sky visible around/behind icons).
    - **Zone C — Nav chevron (y≈555, x≈975, ~65 × 65 px):** floating white circle with a black `›` chevron glyph inside. Same primitive as Anthropic/Yan Liu's white-circle chevrons but slightly larger (~65 px vs ~50 px). Positioned on the right edge, vertically centred with the card's painterly zone. No left chevron (this is slide 1 of the carousel).
    - **Zone D — Display headline (y≈1090 → 1260, x≈60 → 1000):** `How does a VPC actually work?` — sans-serif Bold, ~110 px, `#FFFFFF` pure white, left-aligned, wraps 2 lines:
        - Line 1: `How does a VPC`
        - Line 2: `actually work?`
        - Line-height ~1.0 (very tight).
        - Font candidate: **Inter Bold**, **Söhne Kraftig**, or **Neue Haas Grotesk Display Bold**. Not italic, not serif. Geometric-humanist sans with high x-height.
        - Note the **word `actually`** — a signature nextwork-voice tell. Signals "let me clear up the confusion" — anti-marketing, empathetic, teacher-voice.
    - **Zone E — Brand pill (y≈1275 → 1330, x≈60 → 310, ~250 × 55 px):** small white rounded-pill:
        - Fill `#FFFFFF`.
        - Radius = height/2 (fully rounded).
        - **Left icon (~x 80, y 1290, ~35 × 35 px):** a **hexagon-with-inner-shape mark** — the nextwork logomark. Muted dark teal fill `~#4A5F5A`. Sits vertically centred in the pill.
        - **Right wordmark (~x 130, y 1290):** `nextwork` — sans-serif Bold ~28 px, `#141414` near-black, single word (no space, all lowercase). Same font family as the display headline.
    - **Zone F — Dot indicator (y≈1300 → 1315, centred at x≈540):** row of **11 tiny circular dots**, ~10 px diameter each, ~14 px between centres. First (leftmost) dot filled `#FFFFFF`, remaining 10 dots outlined only (thin white stroke, no fill — muted).
        - Confirms this is **slide 1 of an 11-slide carousel** — largest carousel we've seen so far in the reference set (surpassing Yan Liu's 9-slide carousel).
        - Note the dot indicator is positioned in a **narrow band inside the card**, aligned horizontally with the brand pill vertically (both at y≈1290-1330). Not floating outside the card like Anthropic slides — nextwork keeps ALL chrome inside the card boundary.

- **Grid & alignment:** single-column composition. Icons top-left, headline bottom-left, brand pill bottom-left, chevron mid-right. Everything left-aligned (except the centred dot indicator).

- **Typography:**
    - **Display headline** `How does a VPC actually work?`: sans-serif Bold, ~110 px, white, left-aligned, 2 lines, tight leading. Confirmed the nextwork type stack uses **Inter Bold** or equivalent modern geometric sans as its display voice.
    - **Brand wordmark** `nextwork`: sans-serif Bold ~28 px, near-black on white pill.
    - No other text. Very restrained.

- **Decorative elements:**
    - **AWS service-icon strip** (7 icons) — the visual "table of contents" preview.
    - **Painterly cinematic artwork** — full-bleed inside the card. AI-generated in a very specific oil-painting style. Warmly-lit, soft-edged, no photographic sharpness.
    - **Hexagon logomark** in the brand pill.
    - **Nav chevron** on right edge.
    - **Dot progress indicator** at bottom.

- **Layering order (back → front):**
    - `[dark canvas bg #0D0906]`
    - `[card rounded rect with painterly vineyard artwork]`
    - `[AWS icon strip]`
    - `[nav chevron circle]`
    - `[display headline text (over artwork)]`
    - `[brand pill (over artwork)]`
    - `[dot indicator dots]`

- **Shadows & effects:**
    - Card itself: no drop shadow visible (or very subtle at ~5% opacity — the dark canvas already provides contrast).
    - Nav chevron: soft drop shadow beneath the white circle (~offset y 4, blur 12, colour rgba(0,0,0,0.2)).
    - Brand pill: same subtle drop shadow.
    - Painterly artwork has painterly gradients baked in (not applied as post-effects).
    - No grain, no vignette, no filter — the artwork's dreamlike quality comes from the AI-image-gen style, not from post-processing.

- **Brand markers:**
    - `nextwork` wordmark + hexagon logomark in the brand pill (bottom-left, all slides).
    - AWS service-icon strip signals content authority — reader immediately trusts "this is about real AWS services".
    - The dark-canvas + inset-card composition is itself a brand signature — no other creator in our reference set uses this "framed postcard" convention.

- **Word count:** 6 (headline) + 1 (brand) = 7 words total. Cognitive load = **very low**. Cover slides are meant to be scanned in <2s.

#### Fabric.js reproduction notes
- **Card container:** `Rect` with `rx=44, ry=44, fill='#FFFFFF'` (base), then clipped to hold a full-bleed image. Alternatively, use `clipPath` on the `FabricImage` with a rounded-rect path.
- **Dark canvas bg:** `Rect` filling the canvas with `fill='#0D0906'`.
- **Painterly artwork:** `FabricImage.fromURL(...)`. This is a **hero AI-image-gen asset** — the whole card's visual identity depends on getting the right painterly style. Options for reproduction:
    1. **User provides painterly artwork per slide** — highest quality, requires design pass per slide.
    2. **AI-image-gen with a locked style prompt:** e.g. `"cinematic painterly oil-painting-style illustration, golden-hour lighting, warm earth tones, dreamlike atmosphere, [scene: vineyard with figure walking toward horizon], no text, no logos, painterly brushwork visible, soft edges"`. Would need prompt engineering + curation.
    3. **Curated stock library** of ~30 painterly scenes on the nextwork palette, LLM picks by topic.
    - **Recommend option 2 with a curated fallback library** — the painterly style is highly reproducible with the right prompt.
- **AWS icon strip:** row of `FabricImage` objects. Each icon is a **pre-designed rounded-square asset** — AWS provides brand assets for its services. Ship an **AWS icon library** (30+ icons covering common services: EC2, S3, Lambda, RDS, DynamoDB, CloudWatch, IAM, VPC, CloudFront, Route 53, API Gateway, ECS, EKS, SNS, SQS, etc.) at 85×85 px, ~18 px radius. Store in `backend/assets/icons/aws/`.
- **Display headline:** `Textbox` with `fontFamily='Inter'`, `fontWeight=700`, `fontSize=110`, `textAlign='left'`, `fill='#FFFFFF'`, `lineHeight=1.0`.
- **Brand pill:** compound primitive — rounded `Rect` white fill + centred `FabricImage` hexagon + `Textbox` wordmark. `makeBrandPill({logoUrl, wordmark, textColor, bgColor, height, padding})`.
- **Nav chevron:** existing `makeCircularNavArrow` helper from Anthropic slides — same primitive.
- **Dot indicator:** existing `makeDotProgressIndicator` — with `activeStyle: "fill"` (white filled dot vs outlined dots).
- **New helpers required:**
    - `makeAWSIconStrip({icons: Array<{service: string, url?: string}>, size, gap, radius, x, y})` — the horizontal service-icon row. Takes AWS service keys (e.g. `["aws", "ec2", "s3", "lambda", "rds", "cloudwatch", "iam"]`) and resolves them via the shipped AWS icon registry.
    - `makeNextworkCard({paintingUrl, iconStrip, displayHeadline, brandPill, chevron, dotIndicator, cardInset, cardRadius})` — the full card composition. High-level template builder.
    - `makeBrandPill({logoUrl, wordmark, textColor, bgColor, height, padding})` — reusable brand-pill primitive. Yan Liu could use a variant of this (though her carousel uses profile cards, not brand pills).
- **Font loading:** confirms **Inter Bold** at heavy weights (already established). No new fonts.

#### Motion cue
- **The walking figure creates strong implied motion** — reader eye follows the figure into the vanishing point, then jumps to the headline. Classic "journey" cover.
- **Nav chevron** on right = swipe forward.
- The AWS icon strip is **static but promising** — signals "you'll learn about all these services".

#### IG safe-zone check
- AWS icon strip at y≈60–145 — partially inside the top 200-px avatar-bar risk zone. Icons may be partially clipped by IG chrome on some viewports. **Moderate risk** — recommend production adaptation move icon strip to y≥180.
- Painterly artwork at y≈145–1080 — the figure and horizon are mid-canvas, safely in the content zone.
- Display headline at y≈1090–1260 — safely below the top zone. **First line is above the 1170-px bottom-CTA-overlay risk boundary; second line MAY be partially clipped by IG's caption overlay when expanded.** Two-line headlines on covers are common here — nextwork accepts this trade-off.
- Brand pill + dot indicator at y≈1275–1330 — deep inside the 1170-px risk zone. On covers this is acceptable — the brand pill is a repeated brand marker.

#### Reusability score
- **`makeNextworkCard` template as a whole:** 5/5 — the entire nextwork-carousel-cover composition is genuinely one of the most distinctive layouts in our reference set. Every AWS/cloud/dev-education creator would benefit from this template: cinematic-artwork-hero + tech-service-icon-preview + question-headline + brand-pill.
- **AWS icon strip primitive:** 5/5 — reusable across any AWS-education slide, not just covers. Same primitive with different service selections could preview per-slide service focus.
- **Painterly-artwork-inside-rounded-card composition:** 5/5 — reusable for any brand that wants a "cinematic magazine-cover" aesthetic. Yan Liu could use a variant for her manifesto slides.
- **Brand pill primitive:** 5/5 — every branded slide needs a lightweight brand-marker; the white-pill-with-logomark-and-wordmark is a canonical form.

#### Template pattern classification (final)
- Proposed key: **`aurora-nextwork-cover`** — a canonical "cinematic AWS-education carousel cover" template.
- Structure: `{darkCanvas, card: {inset, radius, paintingUrl, iconStrip, displayHeadline, brandPill, chevron, dotIndicator}}`.
- **Sits in a NEW template family:** `aurora-nextwork-*` — a full brand system distinct from Anthropic (`aurora-product-*`), SahilBloom (`aurora-editorial-*`), personal-brand (Yan Liu subVariant), and compact-family (`aurora-compact-*`).
- **HIGH PRIORITY** for AWS/cloud-education use cases. Also a template family worth considering for **ANY tech-education carousel** — the painterly-artwork + icon-strip + question-headline composition is powerful for cloud, ML, security, database, and general dev-education topics.

#### Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer)
- Display headline formula: **`"How does [Subject] [adverb] [verb]?"`** — a **beginner-empathetic question**, with `actually` as the signature adverb. Examples:
    - `"How does a VPC actually work?"` (this slide)
    - `"How does a database actually scale?"`
    - `"How does IAM actually secure your account?"`
    - `"How does S3 actually store your files?"`
- Voice: **teacher-empathetic**, first-person absent, subject-focused. `actually` is the empathetic tell — signals "I know you've been confused, let me help".
- ~5-8 words, 1-2 lines, ends with `?`.
- Alternative headline formulas (nextwork brand may vary):
    - `"What is [Service]?"` — even simpler beginner framing.
    - `"[Service]: the visual guide"` — more colon-descriptor.
    - `"[Service] explained in [N] minutes"` — time-scoped promise.

#### Notes / open questions
- **This is my first analyzed nextwork/ slide** — the folder-level baseline notes at the top of this file are provisional and will be refined as I analyze more slides. Expect the baseline to stabilise by slides 50-55.
- **The 7 AWS service icons on this cover** preview specific services the carousel will discuss. Their choice (aws, chip/EC2, S3, Lambda, RDS, CloudWatch, IAM) is a **content-planning artefact** — nextwork's team decides "we'll touch these 7 services in this VPC carousel", and the icons signal scope to the reader. **Confirms:** the AWS icon strip is **content-driven per carousel**, not decorative. Our LLM writer should generate `iconStrip: string[]` as part of the carousel's cover-slide spec.
- **The painterly artwork style is nextwork's most distinctive visual signature.** Every carousel needs matching artwork — likely commissioned or AI-generated per topic. This is the **make-or-break asset dependency** for the template family, analogous to SahilBloom's hedcut illustrations and Anthropic's 3D renders.
- **The `actually` in the headline is a subtle but important voice signal.** It differentiates nextwork from more corporate AWS content — signals humanistic teaching voice. Recommend our LLM writer include an `authorial-voice` prop when generating headlines: `voice: "corporate" | "empathetic-teacher" | "playful" | "philosophical"`.
- **Dark canvas + inset card composition is highly distinctive.** No other reference-set creator uses it. Could be adopted as a **premium / cinematic subVariant** across other template families — Anthropic could use it for research-anthology slides, Yan Liu could use it for craft-manifesto slides.
- **Prediction for slides 49+ (nextwork/ body slides):** based on the 11-dot indicator, expect a 10-slide body sequence following this cover. Common AWS-education carousel patterns suggest: intro-definition slide, architecture-diagram slide, step-by-step slides (2-4), security/safety slide, use-case slide, comparison slide, summary/close slide. Each may introduce a new bodyLayout variant for the nextwork template family.

### nextwork/image copy.png
**Pattern class:** essay-body / concept-comparison (introduces the "why" of the topic via a **without-VPC vs with-VPC diagram**).
**Overall vibe (1 line):** Slide 2 of the VPC carousel — same painterly vineyard canvas continues (darkened / desaturated) as ambient bg, with a bold "Why does a VPC exist?" heading + intro paragraph + a **2-column contrast diagram** using AWS EC2 icon + dashed-outlined "VPC" boundary box.

Same nextwork/ folder-level baseline (dark canvas + inset card + painterly artwork + brand pill + dot indicator). Deltas:

- **Card container:** same rounded rect, same inset ~25 px. **Painterly artwork continues** but is now **DARKENED / DESATURATED** (~40% brightness) to serve as backdrop rather than hero. Confirms nextwork's design pattern: **hero-artwork slide (cover, slide 1) → same artwork darkened as ambient backdrop on body slides**. Creates carousel-wide visual continuity — reader always feels they're "still in the vineyard" while learning. **This is a signature nextwork technique** worth reproducing.
- **NO AWS icon strip at top** on this body slide. The icon strip is a cover-only chrome element (slide 1); body slides drop it. Confirms: `iconStrip` is a **cover-slide-only slot** in the nextwork template family.
- **Nav chevrons:** BOTH L + R chevrons visible (y≈390) — same white-circle primitive. Slide 2 gets both directions.
- **Dot indicator:** 11-dot row, **dot 2 filled white**. Confirms 11-slide carousel.

- **Zone A — Display headline (y≈55 → 205, x≈45 → 1000):** `Why does a VPC exist?` — sans-serif Bold ~110 px, `#FFFFFF`, left-aligned, wraps 2 lines (`Why does a VPC` / `exist?`). Same font stack (Inter Bold or Söhne Kraftig) as image 48's headline. **Confirms nextwork's headline stack is stable across cover + body slides.** Voice: teacher-empathetic — asks a "why" question, sets up the answer below.

- **Zone B — Intro paragraph (y≈245 → 385, x≈45 → 1010):** `A VPC is a virtual private cloud, which is your own private network inside AWS. Without one, every server you launch would answer to the whole internet.` — sans-serif Regular ~40 px, `#FFFFFF`, left-aligned, wraps 4 lines, line-height ~1.3. Word count: ~28.
    - **Definition-first structure:** `[Acronym] is [full-expansion], which is [plainspoken-metaphor]. Without one, [negative-consequence].` Classic teacher-explainer form.

- **Zone C — Contrast diagram (y≈400 → 700, x≈40 → 1040):** the new element.
    - **Two-column layout, ~40 px gap:**
        - **Column 1: `without a VPC`** (left).
        - **Column 2: `with a VPC`** (right).
    - **Column-header labels (y≈405):**
        - Left: `without a VPC` — sans-serif Regular ~26 px, white.
        - Right: `with a VPC` — sans-serif Regular ~26 px, colour **mint-green** `~#4AC48D`. **New palette token: `NEXTWORK_ACCENT_MINT`**. Signals "this is the safe/good one".
    - **Row 1 — "the internet" pill (~y 440 → 495):** both columns have identical dark-charcoal rounded-rectangle pill labelled `the internet` — Inter Regular ~26 px white text on dark bg `~#2A2820`. Pill ~440 × 55 px, radius ~14 px.
    - **Row 2 — vertical arrows (~y 500 → 555):**
        - Left column: **thin red/coral vertical arrow** `~#E27168` pointing DOWN. ~4 px stroke, ~50 px tall, small filled arrowhead. **New palette token: `NEXTWORK_ACCENT_WARN`**.
        - Right column: **thin mint-green vertical arrow** `~#4AC48D` pointing DOWN.
    - **Row 3 — EC2 icons + optional VPC boundary (~y 560 → 700):**
        - **Left column: standalone AWS EC2 icon** (~110 × 110 px, orange gradient rounded square `~#F08A3D` with white chip glyph). **No boundary** — exposed to the internet directly. Caption below: `anyone can knock` (Inter Regular ~26 px, white, centred). Human-scale metaphor for vulnerability.
        - **Right column: same EC2 icon INSIDE A VPC BOUNDARY BOX** (~y 550, ~440 × 160 px):
            - **Boundary box:** rounded rect with **thick dashed mint-green stroke** (`~#4AC48D`, stroke ~3 px, dash `[8, 6]`), radius ~18 px, very subtle dark-green tint fill.
            - **Purple hexagon icon + label** (top-left of box, half-above/half-inside): ~30 × 30 px purple hexagon `~#7A5AD8` (the AWS VPC service icon) + `your VPC` label in white Inter Regular ~26 px.
            - **AWS EC2 icon inside the box** — same orange chip icon, now protected.
            - **Caption below the box:** `you decide who gets in` — white, centred. Contrasts left column's `anyone can knock`.

- **Grid & alignment:** headline + paragraph = single-column left-aligned. Diagram = 2-column grid, icons + captions centred within columns.

- **Typography:** all Inter (Regular + Bold), white. Headline ~110 px Bold, paragraph ~40 px Regular, diagram labels ~26 px Regular.

- **Decorative elements:** darkened painterly vineyard backdrop + 2-column contrast diagram (dark pills + coloured arrows + AWS icons + dashed VPC boundary + purple hexagon service marker) + L/R nav chevrons + dot indicator. **No brand pill on this body slide** — confirms brand pill is cover-only in this carousel (or positioned differently on body slides; verify with subsequent slides).

- **Layering (back → front):** `[dark canvas bg]` → `[darkened painterly artwork]` → `[headline + paragraph]` → `[column headers]` → `[dark "the internet" pills]` → `[coloured vertical arrows]` → `[VPC dashed boundary box]` → `[VPC purple hexagon + label]` → `[AWS EC2 icons]` → `[captions]` → `[L/R chevrons]` → `[dot indicator]`.

- **Shadows & effects:** subtle drop shadow on chevrons. No other post-effects.

- **Word count:** 5 (headline) + 28 (intro) + 8 (diagram labels) + 8 (captions) = ~49 words. Cognitive load = medium, justified for a definition + contrast slide.

#### Fabric.js reproduction notes
- **NEW `bodyLayout` variant: `"concept-contrast-2-column"`** for the nextwork family. Structure per column: `{header, sharedInputPill, directionalArrow (colour-coded), mainIcon, optionalBoundaryBox (with service label + hexagon icon), outcomeCaption}`.
- **New Fabric helpers:**
    - `makeConceptContrastDiagram({leftColumn, rightColumn, gap, x, y, width, height})` — 2-column layout builder.
    - `makeContrastColumn({header, pill, arrow: {color, style}, mainIcon, boundaryBox?, caption})` — one column's content.
    - `makeVPCBoundaryBox({label, hexagonIconUrl, dashStroke, dashPattern, cornerRadius, children})` — reusable for ANY "logical grouping" callout (VPC, subnet, availability zone, security group, IAM boundary, K8s namespace, Docker container).
    - `makeArrowVertical({length, color, strokeWidth, arrowheadSize, direction: "up" | "down"})` — simple vertical arrow.
- **Reuse:** `makeAWSIconRegistry` (image 48) provides EC2 + VPC-hexagon icons.
- **Painterly-artwork continuation** — reuse the same asset from slide 1 with a **~40% brightness + ~30% saturation reduction** filter. Fabric: `filters: [new Brightness({brightness: -0.6}), new Saturation({saturation: -0.3})]`. Alternative: ship pre-processed darkened variant per carousel (`artwork.hero` + `artwork.darkened`).

#### Motion cue
- **Vertical arrows imply data flow** — internet → server, colour-coded red (vulnerable) vs green (safe).
- **VPC boundary box** creates containment visual — reader sees "the box protects the server", building the exact mental model the slide wants.

#### IG safe-zone check
- Display headline at y≈55–205 — top inside 200-px avatar-bar risk zone; line 1 will be partially clipped. Same tight-top pattern as image 48.
- Intro paragraph + diagram at y≈245–700 — fully in safe zone.
- The card in this export appears cropped at y≈770 (dot indicator visible there). If the source is a **Reel-format 9:16 export**, this is expected. For the standard 1080×1350 carousel, dot indicator would sit at y≈1290. Verify at asset-inspection time.

#### Reusability score
- **`concept-contrast-2-column` body layout:** 5/5 — universal across before/after, without/with, wrong/right, old/new comparisons.
- **`makeVPCBoundaryBox` primitive:** 5/5 — HIGHEST-VALUE new primitive. Universally reusable for any "logical container" in a system diagram (VPC, subnet, K8s pod, Docker, security group, namespace, database schema).
- **Colour-coded contrast arrows** (red-warn vs green-safe): 5/5 — canonical UX pattern.
- **Darkened-artwork continuation** technique: 5/5 — reusable for any brand with a hero-image cover, creates carousel-wide cohesion.

#### Template pattern classification (final)
- **`aurora-nextwork-body`** with `bodyLayout: "concept-contrast-2-column"`.
- **Confirms `aurora-nextwork-*` as a full template family** with distinct cover + body variants.

#### Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer)
- **Body heading formula:** `"Why does [Subject] exist?"` or `"What problem does [Subject] solve?"` — causal question, sets up definition.
- **Intro-paragraph formula:** `"[Acronym] is [full-expansion], which is [plainspoken-metaphor]. Without one, [negative-consequence]."` — 2-sentence definition + stakes. ~25-35 words.
- **Diagram-caption formula:** plainspoken **outcome statements** (4-6 words), NOT technical language. `anyone can knock` / `you decide who gets in` — door-metaphor beats `accepts inbound traffic on port 80`. LLM should always translate technical outcomes into human-scale metaphors for nextwork-style captions.

#### Notes / open questions
- **Painterly-artwork continues across the carousel** — cover = hero brightness, body = darkened backdrop. Recommend our system: `carousel.heroArtworkUrl` + `carousel.heroArtworkDarkenedUrl` (or auto-generated darkened variant via filter).
- **New palette tokens for the nextwork family:**
    - `NEXTWORK_ACCENT_MINT ≈ #4AC48D` — safe/good/positive.
    - `NEXTWORK_ACCENT_WARN ≈ #E27168` — vulnerable/bad/warning.
    - `NEXTWORK_AWS_ORANGE ≈ #F08A3D` — EC2 icon default fill.
    - `NEXTWORK_VPC_PURPLE ≈ #7A5AD8` — VPC service icon fill.
    - `NEXTWORK_PILL_DARK ≈ #2A2820` — dark pill background for input labels.
- **AWS service icons appear in two contexts:** (1) top icon strip on covers (preview), (2) inline within body diagrams as service markers next to boundary boxes.
- **The `the internet` pill in BOTH columns identically** is a **shared-input pattern** — same source, divergent outcomes. Powerful teaching device. LLM should recognise when a contrast diagram has a shared input vs independent inputs per column.
- **Prediction for slides 50+:** step-by-step configuration, architecture diagrams, use-case scenarios, security best-practices, summary/close. Each may introduce new bodyLayout variants.

_Progress: 49 / 85 complete._

### nextwork/image copy 2.png
**Pattern class:** essay-body / numbered-list-with-connectors (a **"parts of X" enumeration slide** with tiny action-verb captions between items).
**Overall vibe (1 line):** Slide 3 of the VPC carousel — same darkened painterly bg + a **numbered vertical list of 6 VPC components**, each with a coloured bullet dot + name, and tiny lowercase **action verbs** (`split`, `direct`, `open`, `hide`, `allow`) sitting on a vertical rule between items, communicating what each component does to the next.

Same nextwork/ folder-level baseline (dark canvas + inset card + darkened painterly artwork + nav chevrons + dot indicator). Deltas:

- **Card container:** same rounded rect, painterly vineyard darkened as backdrop.
- **NO AWS icon strip at top** (confirmed body-slide convention).
- **NEW top-right corner element (~y 90, x 545, ~65 × 65 px):** the **VPC purple hexagon icon** floats in the top-right corner as a **slide-topic marker**. Same `~#7A5AD8` purple hexagon primitive as image 49's boundary-box marker, now used as a **standalone topic-badge** in the corner of a body slide. Recommend new slot: `slide.topicBadgeIcon` — a top-right service icon that anchors the slide to a specific AWS service.
- **Nav chevrons:** BOTH L + R chevrons visible (y≈400).
- **Dot indicator:** 11-dot row, **dot 3 filled white**. Slide 3 of the carousel.

- **Zone A — Display headline (y≈50 → 200, x≈45 → 500):** `The parts of a VPC` — sans-serif Bold ~100 px, `#FFFFFF`, left-aligned, wraps 2 lines (`The parts` / `of a VPC`). Note the headline is **narrower** than image 48/49's headlines (~500 px wide) — leaves room for the topic-badge in the top-right corner. LLM should account for badge presence when sizing headline.

- **Zone B — Numbered list of VPC components (y≈245 → 715, x≈45 → 700):** the distinctive new element.
    - **6 list items stacked vertically**, ~70 px per item (row-height including inter-item connector).
    - **Anatomy of one list item (using item 1 as reference):**
        - **Number prefix (~x 55):** `1` — sans-serif Regular ~40 px, `#FFFFFF`, plain digit (no circle, no badge, no fill — just the numeral).
        - **Coloured bullet dot (~x 130, y-centred):** small ~14 px diameter filled circle. **Each item has a unique bullet colour** (see palette below).
        - **Component name (~x 160):** e.g. `Subnets` — sans-serif **Bold** ~50 px, `#FFFFFF`, single line.
    - **Bullet colours per item (component-to-colour mapping):**
        1. `Subnets` — **blue** `~#3B7EDC` (sibling of SELECTION_BLUE from claude/image 45).
        2. `Route tables` — **amber/yellow** `~#E8B045`.
        3. `Internet gateway` — **mint-green** `~#4AC48D` (same as image 49's NEXTWORK_ACCENT_MINT).
        4. `NAT gateway` — **purple** `~#B65FE0` (brighter than VPC hexagon purple).
        5. `Security groups` — **orange** `~#F08A3D` (same as image 49's NEXTWORK_AWS_ORANGE).
        6. `Network ACLs` — **white/near-white** `~#F5F0E8` (soft cream).
    - **The colour-coding is deliberate:** each VPC component gets a canonical colour that presumably reappears in subsequent slides. **Reader builds mental colour-associations** — this is a **legend-defining slide**. Recommend our LLM writer emit `carousel.componentColorMap: { subnets: "#3B7EDC", ... }` and re-use consistently across the carousel.
    - **Inter-item connector line + action verb (between items):**
        - **Thin vertical line** ~1 px stroke, `#7A7A7A` muted grey, extending ~30 px from the bottom of one item's row to the top of the next.
        - **Tiny lowercase verb** sits on the line, offset slightly to the right (~x 100, y = midpoint between items): `split`, `direct`, `open`, `hide`, `allow` — sans-serif Regular ~22 px, muted grey `~#8A857B`. Verbs are all-lowercase.
    - **Semantics of the connector verbs:**
        - `1 Subnets → split → 2 Route tables` (subnets split the VPC; route tables are what they route with).
        - `2 Route tables → direct → 3 Internet gateway` (route tables direct traffic; the gateway is the exit).
        - `3 Internet gateway → open → 4 NAT gateway` (the internet gateway opens outward; the NAT gateway is a controlled variant).
        - `4 NAT gateway → hide → 5 Security groups` (NAT gateways hide internal IPs; security groups add another hiding layer).
        - `5 Security groups → allow → 6 Network ACLs` (security groups allow specific traffic; ACLs are a coarser allow-list layer).
    - **This is a "flowing enumeration"** — the list isn't just parts, it's a **causal chain** of "how each part relates to the next". Perfect teaching device — reader reads top-down like an argument, not just a list.

- **Grid & alignment:** headline top-left; topic-badge top-right; enumerated list left-aligned starting ~y 245; connector verbs slightly indented from the numbers.

- **Typography:**
    - **Display headline:** Inter Bold ~100 px, white (slightly smaller than image 48/49's ~110 px to accommodate the topic badge).
    - **Item numbers:** Inter Regular ~40 px, white. Plain numerals, no ordinal decoration.
    - **Item names:** Inter Bold ~50 px, white.
    - **Connector verbs:** Inter Regular ~22 px, muted grey `~#8A857B` (same as `CONNECTOR_HAIRLINE` from claude/image 41). All-lowercase.
    - **Confirmed:** nextwork's type stack = Inter (Regular + Bold), consistent across cover + body slides.

- **Decorative elements:** darkened painterly vineyard + topic-badge (VPC hexagon top-right) + 6-item numbered list with coloured bullets + connector-verb lines + L/R nav chevrons + dot indicator.

- **Layering (back → front):** `[dark canvas]` → `[darkened painterly artwork]` → `[topic-badge hexagon (top-right)]` → `[headline]` → `[item numbers + bullets + names]` → `[inter-item connector lines + verbs]` → `[nav chevrons]` → `[dot indicator]`.

- **Word count:** 5 (headline) + 12 (item names) + 5 (connector verbs) = ~22 words. Cognitive load = **low-medium** — a list slide is meant for scanning, not reading, and the connector verbs are decorative sub-labels.

#### Fabric.js reproduction notes
- **NEW `bodyLayout` variant: `"numbered-list-with-flow-verbs"`** for the nextwork family. Structure: `{topicBadge, headline, items: Array<{number, bulletColor, name, connectorVerbToNext?: string}>}`.
- **New Fabric helpers:**
    - `makeFlowVerbList({items: Array<{number, bulletColor, name, connectorVerb?}>, numberFont, nameFont, verbFont, connectorLineColor, x, y, itemGap})` — builds the whole enumerated list with inter-item connector lines + verbs.
    - `makeTopicBadge({iconUrl, size, position, cornerRadius})` — small floating service-icon badge for the top-right corner of body slides. Reuses AWS icon registry.
- **Reuse:** `makeAWSIconStrip` (cover-only) and `makeAWSIconRegistry` (image 48) provide the service icons.
- No new fonts required.

#### Motion cue
- **The connector verbs** create implied causal motion — reader eye moves top-to-bottom, and each verb signals "then this happens".
- **Colour-coded bullets** invite the reader to remember each component's colour for later slides.
- **Topic-badge in top-right** signals "this slide is about VPC" (in case reader jumped in mid-carousel).

#### IG safe-zone check
- Headline at y≈50–200 — top inside 200-px avatar-bar risk zone. Line 1 clipped on some viewports.
- Topic badge at y≈90 — also inside top risk zone.
- List at y≈245–715 — safely within content zone.
- Dot indicator at y≈770 — again, appears cropped in this export. Verify aspect ratio at asset inspection.

#### Reusability score
- **`numbered-list-with-flow-verbs` body layout:** 5/5 — extremely reusable for any "parts of X" educational slide. AWS services, database indexes, HTTP request lifecycle, ML pipeline stages, deploy pipeline, auth flow steps, etc.
- **Flow-verb connector primitive:** 5/5 — a distinctive teaching device rarely seen elsewhere. Elevates a plain list into a **causal narrative** with minimal ink.
- **Colour-coded component legend** established on a "parts of X" slide, then reused in subsequent slides: 5/5 — critical UX pattern for multi-slide technical carousels.

#### Template pattern classification (final)
- **`aurora-nextwork-body`** with `bodyLayout: "numbered-list-with-flow-verbs"`.
- Companion to `concept-contrast-2-column` (image 49). Both are body-slide variants for the same template family.

#### Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer)
- Headline formula: **`"The parts of [Subject]"`** or **`"[Subject]: the components"`** — declarative title that promises enumeration. 3-5 words. Voice: teacher-authoritative.
- Item name formula: **1-3-word noun phrase** in Title Case (`Subnets`, `Route tables`, `Internet gateway`, `NAT gateway`, `Security groups`, `Network ACLs`). No verbs, no descriptors, just the concrete component names.
- Connector verb formula: **single lowercase verb** (`split`, `direct`, `open`, `hide`, `allow`) — 1 word, all lowercase, describes what item N *does to* the next item's inputs. LLM should pick verbs that create a **causal chain**, not just adjacent labels.
- Item count: 5-7 items works well (this slide has 6). Fewer feels sparse, more overwhelms.

#### Notes / open questions
- **The colour-coded component legend** established here will presumably recur across the carousel's remaining 8 slides (each subsequent slide likely deep-dives on one component using its designated colour). This is a **carousel-level design contract** — the LLM writer must plan the colour palette on slide 3, then honour it across slides 4-11. Recommend our system support `carousel.componentColorMap` as a first-class field.
- **Topic-badge in top-right corner of body slides** is a new nextwork chrome primitive. Signals slide-level topic (the parent AWS service). May appear on subsequent slides too — worth tracking.
- **Component-to-colour mappings for future recall:**
    - Subnets = blue `#3B7EDC`
    - Route tables = amber `#E8B045`
    - Internet gateway = mint `#4AC48D`
    - NAT gateway = purple `#B65FE0`
    - Security groups = orange `#F08A3D`
    - Network ACLs = cream-white `#F5F0E8`
- **The `flow-verb` primitive is nextwork-signature.** Not seen in any other creator's slides in our reference set. Recommend implementing it early as a **differentiated primitive** that only nextwork-style templates use.
- **Prediction for slides 51+:** likely deep-dive slides on each of the 6 components (subnets, route tables, gateways, security groups, ACLs), each reusing the colour from this legend slide. Would use 5-6 slides for deep-dives + 1-2 summary/close slides = fits the 11-slide arc.

_Progress: 50 / 85 complete._

#### Fabric.js reproduction notes
- **NEW `bodyLayout` variant: `"component-deep-dive-with-console-mockup"`** — a slide dedicated to ONE component of a larger system, featuring:
    - **Top hero embed:** AWS Console screenshot mockup (or generic UI mockup).
    - **Component title with coloured bullet + topic-badge:** the slide's anchor, using the carousel-locked component colour.
    - **Explanatory paragraph:** definition + specific technical detail.
- **New Fabric helpers:**
    - `makeAWSConsoleMockup({panelTitle, leftPanel: {label, sublabel, resourceMiniCard}, rightPanel: {label, sublabel, resourceRows: [{zoneLabel, resourceMiniCard}]}, connectorColor, connectorStyle: "L-shape" | "curved"})` — builds a stylized AWS Console preview panel. Reusable for **any AWS Console-style UI mockup** across the carousel (subnets, route tables, security groups, etc. all have similar Console UI layouts).
    - `makeResourceMiniCard({label, borderColor, fillColor, cornerRadius, size})` — the small cyan-outlined pill representing an individual AWS resource inside a Console panel. Reusable.
    - `makeComponentTitleRow({bulletColor, title, topicBadgeIconUrl, x, y})` — coloured bullet + huge title + right-anchored topic badge.
    - `makeConnectorLShape({from: {x, y}, to: {x, y}, stroke, strokeWidth})` — L-shaped orthogonal connector for tree/hierarchy diagrams.
- **Reuse:** all previous nextwork primitives (darkened painterly bg, nav chevrons, dot indicator, AWS icon registry).
- **AWS Console mockup asset strategy:** since the mockup is stylized (not a real screenshot), it can be **fully composed in Fabric primitives** — white rounded card + text + mini-cards + connector lines. No PNG asset required. **This is a big win** for LLM-driven per-slide customisation: LLM specifies the panel structure in JSON, renderer draws it.

#### Motion cue
- **Tree-branch connector lines** create implied containment motion — reader eye follows lines from parent VPC to child subnets, building the "VPC contains subnets" mental model.
- **Component colour-code recall** — reader sees the blue bullet and immediately recalls "this was position 1 in the parts list from slide 3".

#### IG safe-zone check
- AWS Console mockup at y≈70–495 — top ~130 px inside the 200-px avatar-bar risk zone. `Preview` label + `VPC` label may be partially clipped.
- Component title at y≈580–700 — safely within content zone.
- Explanatory paragraph at y≈720–900 — safely above the 1170-px bottom risk zone.
- Dot indicator at y≈950 — visible in this export at unusual position (much higher than expected 1290). Confirms likely Reel-format 9:16 export crop.

#### Reusability score
- **`component-deep-dive-with-console-mockup` body layout:** 5/5 — universally reusable for any technical carousel that walks through the components of a larger system. AWS services (subnets, route tables, security groups, ACLs, gateways, etc.), Kubernetes (pods, services, ingresses, ConfigMaps), database (tables, indexes, foreign keys), auth flows (identity provider, token, session, permissions). One deep-dive slide per component.
- **`makeAWSConsoleMockup` primitive:** 5/5 — HIGHEST-VALUE new primitive for AWS-education content. Fully composable in Fabric primitives (no PNG dependency).
- **`makeResourceMiniCard` primitive:** 4/5 — reusable across any cloud-console UI mockup (Azure, GCP, K8s dashboards all have similar "resource-name pill" patterns).
- **Carousel-locked colour recall** (component-to-colour contract from slide 50 honored here on slide 5): 5/5 — critical UX pattern.

#### Template pattern classification (final)
- **`aurora-nextwork-body`** with `bodyLayout: "component-deep-dive-with-console-mockup"`.
- Companion to `concept-contrast-2-column` (image 49) and `numbered-list-with-flow-verbs` (image 50). Third confirmed body-layout variant for the nextwork family.
- **This is likely the "workhorse" body slide** for AWS-education carousels — nextwork will use this pattern repeatedly, one per component. Predicting 4-5 more instances of this pattern in slides 52-56 (for Route tables, Internet gateway, NAT gateway, Security groups, Network ACLs).

#### Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer)
- **Component title formula:** single noun phrase, 1-3 words (`Subnets`, `Route tables`, `Internet gateway`). Matches the exact wording from the "parts of X" list slide.
- **Explanatory-paragraph formula:** `"[Component] is [metaphor-definition], and [reader-agency clause]. A [specific technical instance] gives you [specific data-point] to [verb-phrase]."` — 2-sentence structure: define + concrete example with real numbers. ~25-40 words.
- **Voice for deep-dive body copy:** Bold weight, second-person "you", concrete numbers (`/16`, `65,536`), no jargon-without-explanation.
- **AWS Console panel content:** LLM should generate real-looking resource names (`nextwork-vpc`, `nextwork-subnet-public1-us-west-2a`) that match AWS naming conventions (kebab-case + service-type-prefix + zone-suffix). Signals authenticity.

#### Notes / open questions
- **AWS Console mockup uses cyan `#0FA8B5` for the resource-mini-card outlines and connector lines** — a new palette token specific to the AWS Console UI aesthetic. Recommend `NEXTWORK_CONSOLE_CYAN = ~#0FA8B5`.
- **Real AWS Console uses a slightly different cyan** (`#00A0B0` or similar); nextwork's version is stylized and slightly more saturated. Acceptable brand liberty.
- **The Console panel is composed BY the design team**, not screenshotted — this is important because it means we can vary the content per slide (different services, different resources) without needing new PNG assets. **HIGH-VALUE finding for our template system.**
- **The connector-line L-shapes** are a canonical AWS Console visualisation pattern — used to show parent-child relationships between resources. Recommend supporting configurable connector styles: `L-shape` (right-angle), `curved` (bezier), `straight` (direct).
- **The component's colour bullet on the deep-dive slide** consistently matches the colour assigned in the "parts of X" slide (slide 3 / image 50). Confirms the **colour-code contract** across the carousel.     - `IGW` is the industry acronym — nextwork explicitly teaches it ("most people shorten it to IGW"). Small but effective teaching move.

- **Grid & alignment:** flow diagram at top (centred). Bullet + title left-aligned in middle. Paragraph left-aligned at bottom.

- **Typography:**
    - **Component title:** Inter Bold ~130 px, white, 2 lines.
    - **Explanatory paragraph:** Inter Bold ~40 px, white, 4 lines.
    - **Diagram labels (pills + subnet-card labels):** Inter Regular ~22-28 px, white for active elements, muted grey for dimmed elements.
    - **VPC label** `your VPC`: Inter Regular ~26 px, white.

- **Decorative elements:** darkened painterly vineyard + top-down architecture flow diagram (internet pill → mint arrow → gateway pill → mint arrow → VPC boundary containing 2 subnet cards with EC2 icons) + coloured bullet + component title + L/R nav chevrons + dot indicator.

- **Layering (back → front):** `[dark canvas]` → `[darkened painterly artwork]` → `[the-internet pill]` → `[downward arrows]` → `[internet-gateway pill (mint outlined)]` → `[VPC boundary dashed box]` → `[VPC hexagon + label]` → `[public-subnet card (mint tinted) + EC2 icon + label]` → `[private-subnet card (dark, dimmed) + EC2 icon + muted label]` → `[coloured bullet]` → `[component title]` → `[paragraph]` → `[nav chevrons]` → `[dot indicator]`.

- **Word count:** 2 (title) + 40 (paragraph) + ~10 (diagram labels: `the internet`, `internet gateway`, `your VPC`, `public subnet`, `private subnet, no route`) = ~52 words total. Cognitive load = **medium** — but the diagram carries most of the meaning.

#### Fabric.js reproduction notes
- **NEW `heroEmbed` sub-variant for the `component-deep-dive` bodyLayout: `"architecture-flow-diagram"`** — a top-down network-topology flow with:
    - **N ordered pill/box "stages"** stacked vertically.
    - **Directional arrows** between stages.
    - **Optional container boundaries** (VPC box, security-group box, availability-zone box) grouping child stages.
    - **Highlighted vs dimmed states per stage** — reader immediately sees which elements are "active" (mint-tinted) vs "walled off" (dark/muted).
- **New Fabric helpers:**
    - `makeArchitectureFlowDiagram({stages: Array<{type: "pill" | "subnet-card" | "container-boundary", label, style: "default" | "highlighted" | "dimmed", children?, arrowToNext?: {color, style}}>, x, y, width, gap})` — top-down flow builder. Each stage can have children (for boundary containers).
    - `makeStagePill({label, size, borderColor, borderWidth, fillColor, style: "default" | "highlighted" | "dimmed", textColor})` — a labelled pill primitive with 3 style states.
    - `makeSubnetCard({label, iconUrl, style: "public" | "private" | "custom", width, height})` — subnet-specific card variant (fill colour + label styling per public/private).
- **Reuse:** `makeVPCBoundaryBox` (image 49), `makeArrowVertical` (image 49), AWS icon registry (image 48).
- **All primitives are Fabric-native (no PNGs)** — same architectural win as image 51's Console mockup. Diagrams are fully composable per LLM spec.

#### Motion cue
- **Top-down flow of arrows + colour progression** (dark → mint highlight → dashed boundary) walks the reader through the data path stage by stage.
- **The dimmed private subnet on the right** creates a **visual "why not" moment** — reader eye lands on it, wonders why it's dimmed, reads the paragraph and gets `"only public because its route table points at that door"`. Genius pedagogical composition: the diagram poses a question, the paragraph answers it.

#### IG safe-zone check
- Flow diagram at y≈70–400 — top ~130 px inside top 200-px risk zone. `the internet` pill may be partially clipped.
- Component title at y≈445–645 — safely within content zone.
- Paragraph at y≈665–860 — safely above the 1170-px bottom risk zone.
- Dot indicator at y≈905 — again unusually high (probably Reel-format 9:16 crop).

#### Reusability score
- **`architecture-flow-diagram` heroEmbed sub-variant:** 5/5 — universally reusable for any technical carousel that needs a top-down data-flow / network-topology visualisation. AWS architecture diagrams, K8s service meshes, auth flow (login → validate → session → resource), API request lifecycle (client → LB → service → DB), data pipeline stages (ingest → transform → load → serve).
- **Highlighted vs dimmed state pattern on flow-diagram elements:** 5/5 — a canonical "here's the active path, here's what's walled off" teaching device.
- **Combination of pill + arrow + boundary-box + child-card primitives:** all reusable individually. The composition is where the value is.

#### Template pattern classification (final)
- **`aurora-nextwork-body`** with `bodyLayout: "component-deep-dive-with-architecture-diagram"` (or generalise as `bodyLayout: "component-deep-dive"` with `heroEmbed.type: "console-mockup" | "architecture-diagram" | "console-screenshot" | "custom"` sub-variant).
- **Recommend generalising the deep-dive template:** the outer structure (dark card + darkened bg + top embed + coloured bullet + title + paragraph) is identical to image 51; only the hero embed varies. Ship as `aurora-nextwork-body` + `bodyLayout: "component-deep-dive"` + `heroEmbed: { type, content }` prop.

#### Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer)
- **Deep-dive-paragraph structural formula (refined from image 51):** `"[Component] is [metaphor-definition + optional acronym-note], and [causal clause with reader agency]. [Related-concept clarification recalling the metaphor]."` — 30-50 words. 3 sentences.
- **Metaphor recall pattern:** if the first sentence introduces a metaphor (`single door`), the second sentence should recall it (`points at that door`). Creates memorable teaching. LLM should thread metaphors through sentences, not switch metaphors within a paragraph.
- **Acronym teaching:** if the component has a common industry acronym (`IGW`, `NAT`, `ACL`, `RDS`), the paragraph should include a phrase like `"most people shorten it to [acronym]"` or `"often called [acronym]"`. Small but effective authority signal.
- **Architecture-diagram element labels:** short noun phrases (`the internet`, `internet gateway`, `your VPC`, `public subnet`, `private subnet, no route`). Lowercase for infrastructure elements; the optional descriptor after a comma (`no route`) is a **state qualifier**.

#### Notes / open questions
- **The dimmed / walled-off private subnet** is a beautifully-designed visual teaching device. Reader gets an implicit "if you want the private subnet to have internet, you need something else (a NAT gateway)". The slide **sets up the next slide** (which is likely the NAT gateway deep-dive) without needing to say it explicitly. Confirms nextwork carousels use **cross-slide narrative continuity** — each slide plants a seed for the next.
- **Confirms carousel-locked colour contract holds for 3 slides in a row** (blue Subnets on slide 5, and mint Internet gateway on slide 7). Slide 6 (missing from our export) is presumably `Route tables` in amber `#E8B045`.
- **The `NEXTWORK_ACCENT_MINT` colour serves TRIPLE duty on this slide:** (1) internet-gateway pill outline + fill tint, (2) directional arrows in the flow, (3) component bullet dot. This is deliberate — the whole slide's visual identity is unified by the mint = "internet gateway" contract.
- **The muted private-subnet card uses no distinct new colour token** — it just reuses the `NEXTWORK_PILL_DARK` + `~#8A857B` muted grey for the label. Recommend our system treat "dimmed state" as a compositional style (`opacity: 0.5` or `label-color: muted`) rather than a separate colour token.
- **Prediction for slides 53-56:** likely more component deep-dive slides using either the `console-mockup` or `architecture-diagram` heroEmbed. Slide 8 (NAT gateway) especially interesting since the slide 7 diagram already teased it.

**Template classification:** `aurora-nextwork-body` + `bodyLayout: "component-deep-dive-with-console-mockup"` (same as image 51) — **BUT** introduces one new reusable sub-primitive: the **handwritten annotation callout** (`makeHandwrittenAnnotation`). Everything else is a repeat of the pattern already documented at full depth for image 51.

**What is unchanged from image 51 (do not re-document):** dark canvas + inset rounded card, darkened painterly artwork continuing the vineyard scene, AWS Console mini-mockup as heroEmbed at the top, nav chevrons (◄ ►) at mid-left/right, coloured bullet + component title + paragraph in the bottom half, 11-dot indicator at bottom-centre (slide 8 active).

**Console mockup content (this slide):** a Security-group rules table with 4 columns (`Type ▼ | Protocol ▼ | Port range ▼ | Source`) and 3 rows: `HTTPS / TCP / 443 / ::/0`, `HTTP / TCP / 80 / pl-0ebdb9eee4d136317 (enterprise-…)` (with a blue hyperlink-styled Source), `HTTPS / TCP / 443 / 0.0.0.0/0` — the third row is **outlined in the warn-red colour** (`NEXTWORK_ACCENT_WARN #E27168`), marking it as the "insecure rule" the annotation points to. Column headers have a subtle chevron `▼` marker indicating sortable. Table is on white/near-white bg — same style as image 51's console.

**Component-title row (unchanged pattern):**
- **Bullet:** orange dot `#F08A3D` (`NEXTWORK_AWS_ORANGE`) at ~28 px diameter — matches the locked carousel colour-contract for Security groups.
- **Title:** `Security groups` — Inter Bold, ~130 px, white, at y≈475.
- **Paragraph** at y≈590–770, Inter Bold ~40 px white, 3 lines: `"A security group is a firewall wrapped around one server, and it only ever holds rules that allow traffic in."` — 21 words. Follows the same deep-dive-paragraph formula: `[Component] is [metaphor-definition], and [causal clause with reader agency].`

**NEW SUB-PRIMITIVE — Handwritten annotation callout (`makeHandwrittenAnnotation`):**
- **Positioning:** at y≈310–390, immediately below the console mockup and above the nav chevrons. Sits in the dark area (between the console table and the title zone).
- **Anatomy:** (a) a curved hand-drawn arrow in warn-red `#E27168`, ~2.5-3 px stroke, arcing upward from the annotation text to point at the highlighted table row's warn-red outline; (b) an italic **handwritten-style** font label reading `"this one lets in the whole internet"` in warm-cream/off-white `~#F5E8D8` colour, 2 lines, ~36-42 px.
- **Font choice:** appears to be a handwritten script — likely `Caveat`, `Homemade Apple`, or `Kalam` (Google Fonts). Italic slant, uneven letter spacing, casual not calligraphic. Two-line wrap: line 1 `"this one lets in the"`, line 2 `"whole internet"`, roughly centre-aligned under the arrow origin.
- **Semantic role:** annotates a specific row/cell of the mockup with human commentary. The two elements (arrow + handwritten text) function as one composable primitive — pass in `{target: <mockup-child-id>, text: string, position: "below" | "above" | "left" | "right"}` and it should auto-route the curved arrow.
- **Colour semantics:** the annotation uses the warn-red `NEXTWORK_ACCENT_WARN` for the arrow (matches the highlighted row's outline) — creating a **colour-linkage** between the annotation and its target. Text uses cream not warn-red — keeps it readable against the dark bg.
- **Reusability:** 5/5 — an incredibly transferable primitive. Applicable to code screenshots ("this line has a bug"), UI mockups ("this button is missing"), data visualisations ("outlier here"), architecture diagrams ("bottleneck"). Should ship as a first-class Fabric helper.

**Fabric.js reproduction note:** on top of the image-51 helper set (`makeAWSConsoleMockup`, `makeComponentTitleRow`, `makeResourceMiniCard`), add:
- `makeHandwrittenAnnotation({text, targetId, color, position, fontFamily = "Caveat"})` — renders an italic handwritten `IText` + a curved `Path` arrow computed from the annotation's centre to the target element's bounding-box edge. Curve should use a quadratic Bezier for the "hand-drawn arc" feel. Arrowhead at target end.
- Extend `makeResourceMiniCard` / table-row helper to accept an `outlined: "warn" | "success" | null` prop so a row can be visually flagged.

**Colour semantics summary for this slide:** the `NEXTWORK_ACCENT_WARN` `#E27168` is now confirmed as the **"insecure / dangerous / open-to-all"** semantic colour in the nextwork system (used on VPC-outside boundary in image 49, and now on the `0.0.0.0/0` rule row + annotation arrow here). Locked contract.

**IG safe-zone check:** all critical elements between y≈310 (annotation) and y≈900 (dot indicator) — comfortably inside safe zone. Console mockup at y≈120–290 partially in top risk zone; acceptable since it's a supporting reference.

**Reusability score:** 4/5 (the whole slide) — repeat pattern, but the annotation primitive it introduces is 5/5 on its own.

**Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer):** short deep-dive-paragraph, same formula as slide 51. Handwritten annotations should be **5-10 word conversational asides** — human-voice, lowercase, present tense (`"this one lets in the whole internet"`, `"don't use this in prod"`, `"forget this exists"`). Never end with a period. Never longer than 2 wrapped lines.

**Prediction confirmed:** the carousel-locked colour contract holds for a 4th slide — orange `#F08A3D` for Security groups matches the palette declared in the image-49 analysis. High confidence the remaining component slides (Route tables amber, NAT gateway purple, Network ACLs cream) will follow suit.

### Image 54 — `nextwork/image copy 6.png` (Network ACLs) — REDUCED DEPTH (hybrid layout + new comparison-card primitive)

**Template classification:** `aurora-nextwork-body` + **NEW `bodyLayout: "component-deep-dive-with-comparison-diagram"`** — a HYBRID of the `component-deep-dive` outer pattern (image 51) and the `concept-contrast-2-column` diagram (image 49), but with a fresh internal diagram primitive.

**Slide reads:** Component title `Network ACLs` at top (with cream-white bullet `#F5F0E8` matching the locked carousel colour contract — 5th slide in a row where the colour contract holds), paragraph below title, **2-column comparison-card diagram at the bottom** contrasting security group (stateful) vs network ACL (stateless).

**Layout order (top → bottom):**
1. **Component-title row** (y≈70–200): cream-white bullet `#F5F0E8` + `Network ACLs` title in Inter Bold ~130 px white. No topic-badge icon.
2. **Explanatory paragraph** (y≈220–410, ~4 lines): `"A network access control list, or ACL, guards a whole subnet and forgets every request the moment it passes. A security group remembers, so the reply is allowed back on its own."` — 36 words, same Inter Bold ~40 px white as image 51/52. Explicit **acronym teaching** (`"or ACL"`) and **metaphor pair** (`forgets` vs `remembers`) — signature nextwork paragraph formula.
3. **Nav chevrons** (y≈450) — flanking the comparison diagram.
4. **2-column comparison-card diagram** (y≈480–730).
5. **Dot indicator** (y≈780) — 11 dots, dot 9 filled. Confirms slide 9.

**NEW SUB-PRIMITIVE — `makeRequestFlowComparisonCard`:**
- Each of the 2 columns is a **rounded-rectangle card** (~440 × 260 px, radius ~22 px). The two cards have contrasting fills:
  - **Left card (security group):** subtle **orange-tinted dark fill** (~`#2E1F16` — very dark warm brown), thin orange `#F08A3D` outline. The security group is nextwork's "stateful, remembers, allows back" version.
  - **Right card (network ACL):** subtle **cream/neutral-tinted dark fill** (~`#2A2620` — near-neutral dark), thin cream `#F5F0E8` outline. The network ACL is "stateless, forgets, blocks reply".
- **Card anatomy (both cards identical layout, colours swap):**
  - **Title row** (top of card): main label in the card's accent colour (`security group` in orange `#F08A3D` / `network ACL` in cream `#F5F0E8`), Inter Bold ~34 px. Below it, a small **italic sub-label** `it remembers` / `it forgets` in matching-colour Inter Italic ~24 px. This is a **tone-of-voice sub-label** — 2-word personality description of the component.
  - **Request-in row** (mid-card upper): tiny white Inter Regular ~22 px label `request in`, with a **mint-green horizontal arrow** `#4AC48D` (~3 px stroke, arrowhead pointing right) beneath it, spanning most of the card width. Both cards get an identical mint request-in arrow — the input is the same in both scenarios.
  - **Vertical divider line** through the middle of both cards (~1 px muted-grey stroke) — visually splits "request-in phase" from "reply-out phase".
  - **Reply-out row** (mid-card lower):
    - **Left card (security group):** tiny label `reply out`, then a **mint-green horizontal arrow pointing LEFT** (reversed direction) — signals the reply flows back through the same stateful firewall.
    - **Right card (network ACL):** tiny label `reply out`, then a **muted warn-red horizontal arrow pointing LEFT** `#E27168` with a **red X marker** in the middle of the arrow (~`#E27168` X-glyph, ~30 px, drawn as two crossing lines) — visually blocks the reply.
  - **Outcome caption** (bottom of card): in accent colour, Inter Bold ~26 px:
    - Left: `allowed on its own` (mint-green `#4AC48D` colour to match the arrows).
    - Right: `blocked by default` (cream/near-white `#F5F0E8`).

**Semantic contract of the diagram:** teaches **stateful vs stateless** in a single glance. Reader sees: same input (green request-in arrow in both cards), different reply behaviour (green vs red-X). No jargon needed — the visual carries "stateful firewall = remembers = auto-allows reply" vs "stateless firewall = forgets = manual reply rule needed".

**Fabric.js helpers to add:**
- `makeRequestFlowComparisonCard({title, subtitle, accentColor, requestInArrow, replyOutArrow: {color, blocked?: boolean}, outcomeCaption})` — the whole card, composable per column.
- `makeHorizontalArrow({length, color, strokeWidth, direction: "left" | "right", blockedMarker?: {glyph: "x" | "!" | "?", color}})` — horizontal-arrow primitive with optional block/warning marker overlay.
- Extends the `concept-contrast-2-column` outer layout with `interiorCardType: "shared-input-diverge" | "stateful-stateless-flow" | "custom"` variants.

**Colour semantics:**
- The 2 cards use their **component's carousel-locked colour** as accent (orange for Security groups, cream for Network ACLs) — reinforcing the colour contract established on slide 3.
- Green `#4AC48D` = "allow / permit / flowing" (used on request-in and successful reply-out arrows).
- Red `#E27168` = "block / dangerous / blocked" (used on the X-marked blocked reply-out arrow).
- Both are established `NEXTWORK_ACCENT_*` tokens from image 49 — no new colours needed.

**Reusability score:**
- **`component-deep-dive-with-comparison-diagram` bodyLayout:** 5/5 — universally reusable when a slide's job is to teach one component **by contrasting it with a peer**. Not just security groups vs ACLs — cache vs database, put vs post, sync vs async, HTTP vs HTTPS, TCP vs UDP, stateful vs stateless services in general.
- **`makeRequestFlowComparisonCard` primitive:** 5/5 — canonical "input phase → decision → output phase" flow-card for teaching any request-response protocol.
- **The `it remembers` / `it forgets` italic sub-label** technique: 4/5 — cheap way to inject **personality** into a technical comparison. Recommend supporting `subtitle` as an optional italic tone-of-voice slot in comparison-card primitives.

**Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer):**
- **Comparison-diagram title formula:** short component names (`security group` / `network ACL`) — matches the exact wording of the parent components in the "parts of X" list slide.
- **Italic sub-label formula:** 2-word personality descriptor, present tense, third-person (`it remembers` / `it forgets`, `it opens` / `it closes`, `it caches` / `it fetches`). Injects human voice.
- **Outcome-caption formula:** 3-4 word plain-language outcome (`allowed on its own` / `blocked by default`). NEVER use technical language here — this is the visual "punchline" the reader should absorb without effort.
- **Explanatory-paragraph formula:** stick with the deep-dive formula but include the **contrast setup** (`"[Component A] [does X]. [Component B] [does opposite Y], so [consequence]."`). LLM should recognise when a deep-dive paragraph pairs naturally with a peer-comparison and use this structure.

**Notes / open questions:**
- **This is the 6th nextwork body slide analyzed and the 5th confirmed colour-contract match** (cream-white for Network ACLs). Contract holds 5-for-5. Fully validated as a carousel-level design commitment.
- **The slide pairs with slide 8 (Security groups)** — reader has already met Security groups on the previous slide, so the comparison card feels earned rather than dropped-in. Confirms nextwork uses **narrative pairing** across consecutive slides.
- **The "italic sub-label as personality descriptor" is a nextwork signature** — small but effective humanistic touch. Recommend our LLM writer treat `subtitle` as a first-class prop for any comparison primitive.
- **The X-marker on the blocked reply arrow** is a **new sub-primitive** worth adding to our arrow helper. Same pattern as flag/warning glyphs on data-viz arrows — very reusable.
- **This is the 3rd confirmed nextwork bodyLayout that combines an outer template with a swappable interior diagram.** Confirms the template's inner "hero embed" slot is the primary axis of variation. Recommend generalising `heroEmbed.type` as the top-level content prop for the whole `aurora-nextwork-body` family.

### Image 55 — `nextwork/image copy 7.png` ("You already have one" — default-VPC reveal) — REDUCED DEPTH (new bodyLayout variant + minor primitive additions)

**Template classification:** `aurora-nextwork-body` + **NEW `bodyLayout: "reveal-with-console-proof"`** — a "milestone / here's-the-proof" slide, structurally an INVERSION of the component-deep-dive: **headline + paragraph on TOP, console mockup at the BOTTOM with a handwritten annotation pointing at it.** Slide 10 of the carousel (dot 10 filled).

**Slide reads:** the carousel's **narrative reveal** — the reader has just been walked through the 6 VPC components, and this slide flips the emotional register from "here's how it works" to "you already have one; go look". The console mockup shows the user's actual default-VPC subnets list as proof.

**Layout order (top → bottom):**
1. **Display headline** (y≈70–260, x≈45–650, ~600 × 190 px): `You already have one` — Inter Bold ~130 px white, 2 lines (`You already` / `have one`). **Declarative, not a question** — a shift from the "How does…?" / "Why does…?" style headlines earlier in the carousel. This is a **payoff headline** for the reveal moment.
2. **NEW twin-icon topic-badge** (top-right, ~x 870–1030, y 85–225): a **vertical stack of 2 rounded-square icons**:
   - Top icon: cloud-with-lock glyph on **purple/violet** `~#7A5AD8` rounded square (~85 × 85 px, radius ~18 px) — represents the AWS **login / account / IAM identity** context. Not a service the carousel had introduced before, but visually consistent with the AWS icon strip primitive.
   - Bottom icon: **`aws` wordmark tile** (~85 × 85 px, radius ~18 px, near-white bg with black AWS wordmark). Same tile as image 48's cover icon strip.
   - Together they signal: *"log into your AWS account"* — a mini contextual instruction. This is a **new topic-badge variant**: `topicBadge.style = "vertical-icon-stack"` instead of the single hexagon icon of image 50/51. Extend the `makeTopicBadge` helper accordingly.
3. **Explanatory paragraph** (y≈285–500, ~4 lines): `"Every AWS account opens with a default VPC in each Region, at 172.31.0.0/16, and it costs nothing to build your own with a public subnet and a private subnet."` — Inter Bold ~40 px white, 40 words. Contains a specific **default-CIDR data point** (`172.31.0.0/16`) — reinforces the "yours is already there" claim. Voice: reassuring, low-stakes ("costs nothing").
4. **Nav chevrons** (y≈455) — flanking the console mockup.
5. **AWS Console mockup** (y≈490–780, x≈45–580, ~535 × 290 px): **positioned bottom-LEFT** (not full-width, not centred). White rounded card `#FFFFFF`, subtle drop shadow, radius ~16 px. Inside:
   - Header `Subnets (9)` in Inter Bold ~28 px black + sublabel `Subnets within this VPC` in ~14 px grey — same primitive as image 51's Console mockup header.
   - Availability-zone group `us-west-2a` label in Inter Bold ~22 px black, followed by 3 resource-mini-cards stacked vertically:
     - `nextwork-subnet-public1-us-west-2a` — **outlined in warn-red** (`#E27168` stroke, ~2 px, white fill).
     - `nextwork-subnet-private4-us-west-2a` — cyan outlined (`#0FA8B5`, default state).
     - `nextwork-subnet-private1-us-west-2a` — cyan outlined.
   - `us-west-2b` label, followed by `nextwork-subnet-public2-us-west-2b` — also **outlined in warn-red**.
   - **Curved L-shape connector lines in cyan** (`#0FA8B5`) angling from left to link the rows (same primitive as image 51's tree-branch connector).
   - **The warn-red outline on 2 of the mini-cards** is a **new visual highlight state** — signals "these are your public subnets (matter for reveal)". Extends `makeResourceMiniCard` to accept `outlined: "warn" | "success" | "default" | null` — same extension noted for image 53.
6. **Handwritten annotation callout** (y≈540–620, x≈600–850): the same `makeHandwrittenAnnotation` primitive from image 53, this time positioned to the RIGHT of the console mockup. Text: `"yours is already in there"` in cream-white italic handwritten script (~40 px, `~#F5E8D8`), 2 lines. Arrow: a curved warn-red `#E27168` line arcing left from the annotation to point at the warn-red-outlined mini-cards. Confirms `position: "right"` variant of the annotation primitive.
7. **Dot indicator** (y≈820) — 11 dots, dot 10 filled. Confirms slide 10 (penultimate slide).

**Composition semantics:** the FULL-CANVAS visual rhythm is `[headline top-left] + [twin-icon top-right] + [paragraph mid-left] + [mockup bottom-left] + [annotation bottom-right]` — a **Z-pattern reveal**. Eye moves top-left → top-right → paragraph → bottom-left proof → bottom-right punchline. The handwritten annotation is the **emotional payoff** ("yours is already in there") delivered in the same voice as slide 53's "this one lets in the whole internet".

**What's NEW vs already documented primitives:**
- **`reveal-with-console-proof` bodyLayout** — headline + paragraph TOP, console mockup + annotation BOTTOM. Reusable for any "here's the proof it works / here's your actual state" reveal slide near the end of an educational carousel.
- **Twin-icon stacked topic-badge variant** — 2 vertically-stacked square icons in the top-right corner instead of a single hexagon. Signals a contextual instruction ("go log in") rather than a component identity.
- **Console-mockup positioned bottom-LEFT** (not top / not full-width) — new layout position for the mockup primitive. `makeAWSConsoleMockup` already supports arbitrary `{x, y, width, height}`.
- **Warn-red highlight state on individual resource mini-cards** — same extension flagged for image 53; now confirmed as a repeatedly-useful visual state.
- **Handwritten annotation used a 2nd time** — confirms it as a repeatable **teaching-punctuation** primitive that shows up multiple times in a nextwork carousel, especially near mockups.

**Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer):**
- **Reveal-headline formula:** short declarative statement in 2nd-person or possessive (`"You already have one"`, `"It's already running"`, `"You don't need to build this"`) — 3-5 words, Bold, delivers an emotional payoff. Usually near the end of a technical carousel.
- **Reveal-paragraph formula:** `"Every [Entity] [comes-with / opens-with / includes] a [Default X] in [Scope], at [specific data-point], and [reassurance clause]."` — declaration + technical data + reassurance. ~35-45 words.
- **Handwritten annotation formula (repeat):** 4-6 word conversational punchline (`"yours is already in there"`, `"this one lets in the whole internet"`) — human voice, lowercase, present tense.

**Reusability score:** 5/5 for the `reveal-with-console-proof` bodyLayout — every educational carousel benefits from an "and by the way, you already have this / this is running by default" reveal slide near the end. Universal pedagogical device.

**IG safe-zone check:** headline top ~70 px partially inside top risk zone (line 1 may clip on some viewports); twin-icon badge also in top risk zone. Paragraph + mockup + annotation all safely mid-canvas. Dot indicator at y≈820 (Reel-format crop again).

**Notes / open questions:**
- **Confirms:** nextwork carousels have a **narrative arc structure**: cover (Q: "How does X work?") → concept-contrast (why it matters) → parts-of-X list → component deep-dives → **reveal** ("you already have one") → probably summary. The reveal slide is a distinct **narrative role** — recommend our LLM writer support `slide.narrativeRole: "cover" | "setup" | "enumeration" | "deep-dive" | "reveal" | "summary"` as a first-class field.
- **The 2 warn-red-outlined subnets in the mockup** — likely nextwork visually flagging that `public` subnets are the ones the reader should care about (since the earlier slides emphasised public-subnet routing). The colour reuses `NEXTWORK_ACCENT_WARN` but with a different semantic meaning here: **"look at these"** rather than "danger". Recommend our system separate `mini-card.outline.color` from `mini-card.outline.semantic` — the same red can mean warning OR attention.
- **Prediction for slide 11 (final):** almost certainly a summary/recap slide, possibly bringing back all 6 component colours + a "you're ready to build one" CTA + maybe a nextwork brand pill / follow-CTA.
- **Twin-icon topic-badge convention** — worth checking on later slides whether nextwork uses different icon-stack combos for different narrative moments (login flow, deploy flow, cleanup flow).
