### Image 56 — `nextwork/image copy 8.png` (Close-CTA slide with product-mockup) — REDUCED DEPTH (final slide of the carousel + 3 new primitives)

**Template classification:** `aurora-nextwork-body` + **NEW `bodyLayout: "close-cta-with-product-mockup"`** — the **final slide** of the 11-slide VPC carousel (dot 11 filled). Its job is to convert the reader from "I've learned about VPCs" to "I'll go do a hands-on project at nextwork.ai". Introduces **3 new reusable primitives**: inline text-highlight pill, browser-window product mockup, and URL-as-CTA pill.

**Slide narrative role:** `close-cta` — the last-slide CTA that turns education into signup. Distinct from the `reveal` role (image 55, penultimate slide).

**Layout order (top → bottom):**
1. **Display headline with inline highlight** (y≈100–290, x≈45–1015): `Want to learn through free hands-on projects?` — Inter Bold ~100 px white, 3 lines. **The word `free` is wrapped in a NEW inline text-highlight pill** — see next section.
2. **Only LEFT nav chevron visible** (y≈400) — no right chevron because this is the final slide of the carousel. Confirms nextwork chrome adapts to slide position (`slide.isFirst → hide-left-chevron`, `slide.isLast → hide-right-chevron`).
3. **Website / product-mockup** (y≈305–680, x≈75–990, ~915 × 375 px) — a large fake-browser screenshot of the nextwork.ai app.
4. **URL-as-CTA pill** (y≈690–760, centred at x≈540, ~270 × 65 px): `nextwork.ai` in Inter Bold ~34 px white on a **dark rounded pill** `#141414` (nearly black), radius = height/2, no visible border. Sits centred beneath the browser mockup — reads as a "click to visit" affordance.
5. **Dot indicator** (y≈810) — 11 dots, **dot 11 filled**. Confirms this is the final slide, closing the 11-slide carousel.

**NEW SUB-PRIMITIVE 1 — Inline text-highlight pill (`makeInlineHighlightPill`):**
- **Anatomy:** the word `free` is enclosed in a **yellow rounded-rectangle pill** — fill `~#E4C93C` (mustard/highlighter yellow, `NEXTWORK_ACCENT_HIGHLIGHT`), radius ~14 px, ~140 × 90 px. Thin dark outline stroke (`~#8A7420`) at ~1.5 px for definition against the dark bg. Padding around the word: ~18 px horizontal, ~10 px vertical.
- **Text inside the pill:** the word `free` rendered in Inter Bold ~90 px (same size as the surrounding headline), colour `#141414` near-black (inverted from the surrounding white). The word visually sits inline with the rest of the headline as if it's part of the sentence flow — the pill acts like a **prose highlighter** rather than a separate element.
- **Baseline behaviour:** the pill's centre aligns with the surrounding text baseline, so `free` reads as an intra-sentence emphasis marker.
- **Semantic role:** call out a **key modifier** in the headline (usually a price / freeness / risk-free / instant / free / new / etc. adjective). Signals "this is the persuasive word — pay attention to it".
- **Reusability:** 5/5 — universally reusable for any CTA / marketing headline that needs to accent a single word. `"Learn Python in 30 days"` with `free` highlighted, `"Get 10x faster deploys"` with `10x` highlighted, `"Ship code with confidence"` with `confidence` highlighted. Should ship as `makeInlineHighlightPill({word, fillColor, textColor, padding, cornerRadius, borderColor?, fontFamily, fontSize})`.

**NEW SUB-PRIMITIVE 2 — Browser-window product mockup (`makeBrowserWindowMockup`):**
- Distinct from `makeAWSConsoleMockup` (image 51). This is a **generic web-app-in-a-browser-window** primitive:
  - **Outer chrome:** white rounded-rect card (~915 × 375 px, radius ~14 px), subtle drop shadow.
  - **Title-bar row** (top ~55 px): 3 traffic-light dots on the left (`#FF6960` red, `#FCBD3F` yellow, `#29CA43` green — same real macOS colours) at ~x 100 y 335. Centred URL pill: `nextwork.ai` label in Inter Regular ~26 px on a very-light-grey rounded pill (`#EFEFED` bg, ~250 × 42 px, radius ~10 px). No back/forward icons on this variant.
  - **Content area** (below title bar): fills the rest of the card. In this slide, the content is a **6-row × 4-column grid of project-category cards** (~24 cards visible). Each card has a small painterly thumbnail (~150 × 100 px) + category name in Bold + tiny "N Projects" sublabel below. Category names shown: `All`, `New`, `All AI / ML`, `Claude`, `AI Second Brain`, `Productivity`, `Build with OpenClaw`, `Claude Code`, `Generative AI Developer`, `Prompt Engineering`, `AI Tooling`, `Beginner AI Projects`, `RAG API`, `Model Context...`, `[cut off]`, `n8n`. Above the grid: a small filter-tab strip `ALL | ROADMAPS | SPECIALTY | TOOLS` at ~x 100 y 400 with active-tab underline on `ALL`.
  - **Bottom footer** (~y 640): small `Explore` icon + `Your Library` icon + profile-dot — a mini-app tabbar. Confirms this is a stylised representation of the nextwork.ai project-browsing UI.
- **Primitive signature:** `makeBrowserWindowMockup({url, titleBarStyle: "macos" | "chrome" | "safari", contentEmbed: FabricGroup, width, height, x, y})` — accepts an arbitrary Fabric Group as `contentEmbed`, so any UI mockup can slot into the browser window.

**NEW SUB-PRIMITIVE 3 — URL-as-CTA pill (`makeURLCTAPill`):**
- Same shape as the brand pill from image 48 but **inverted colour scheme + no logomark**:
  - Fill: near-black `#141414`.
  - Text: `nextwork.ai` in white Inter Bold ~34 px.
  - Radius: height/2.
  - Padding: ~30 px horizontal.
  - **Function:** implicit "visit this URL" CTA. Reader is supposed to tap/type the URL.
- **Reusability:** 5/5 — every CTA carousel needs a shareable URL pill. Reuse for any Instagram-format close-slide.

**Palette additions:**
- `NEXTWORK_ACCENT_HIGHLIGHT ≈ #E4C93C` — mustard-yellow inline-highlight pill background.
- `NEXTWORK_URL_DARK ≈ #141414` — the dark near-black used on the URL pill (already exists as the general dark text colour token).
- macOS traffic-light colours: `#FF6960` / `#FCBD3F` / `#29CA43` — for the browser-window chrome.

**Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer):**
- **Close-CTA headline formula:** `"Want to [Aspirational Verb] [Highlighted Modifier] [Concrete Deliverable]?"` — question format, 6-8 words, one word wrapped in the highlight pill. Examples:
  - `"Want to learn through free hands-on projects?"` (this slide)
  - `"Want to ship code 10x faster?"` (highlight `10x`)
  - `"Ready to build your first AI agent?"` (highlight `first`)
- Voice: **direct, second-person, question**. Turns the carousel's education into a personal invitation.
- **URL-CTA text formula:** just the domain, no `https://`, no path, no marketing phrase. Trust the pill's visual affordance to communicate "go here".

**Reusability score:**
- **`close-cta-with-product-mockup` bodyLayout:** 5/5 — universal for any educational / product carousel's final slide. AWS-education, coding bootcamps, SaaS product marketing, newsletter signups.
- **All 3 new primitives (highlight pill + browser mockup + URL pill):** 5/5 each — reusable independently across any brand's CTA slide.
- **The full slide is a masterclass in "carousel close" design** — one persuasive question + visual proof of the product + URL. Nothing wasted.

**IG safe-zone check:** headline y≈100–290 straddles the top risk zone (line 1 near y=100 may clip). Browser mockup + URL pill + dot indicator all safely mid-canvas. `only-left-chevron` at y≈400 confirms conventional final-slide chrome adaptation.

**Notes / open questions:**
- **This CLOSES the VPC carousel (slides 48–56 = the 9 slides we have from the 11-slide series; slides 4 and 6 are missing from our export).** Everything from image 48 to 56 is one coherent 11-slide narrative arc.
- **The nextwork.ai product is educational courseware / project-based-learning** — the browser mockup reveals nextwork isn't just an Instagram brand but has a full learning platform behind it. Recommend our system model `carousel.product.mockupUrl` as a first-class slot for the close-CTA slide.
- **The `nextwork.ai` URL pill appears TWICE** on this slide (once in the browser title bar, once as the standalone CTA pill below). Reinforces the domain in two visual registers — subtle marketing craft.
- **The traffic-light macOS chrome is a signal:** nextwork wants readers to trust the product as "a real app you'd use on your Mac / laptop". Recommend supporting `titleBarStyle: "macos" | "chrome" | "safari"` variants of the browser primitive so brands can match their audience's platform.
- **Carousel-level narrative arc — FULLY MAPPED:** cover (slide 1) → concept-contrast (slide 2) → parts-of-X (slide 3) → [missing slide 4] → subnets deep-dive (5) → [missing slide 6] → internet-gateway deep-dive (7) → security-groups deep-dive (8) → network-ACLs deep-dive (9) → "you already have one" reveal (10) → close-CTA (11). Confirmed 11-slide structure with 5 recurring narrative roles: cover, setup, enumeration, deep-dive-per-component, reveal, close-CTA.

**Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer):**
- **Listicle-with-money-hook headline formula:** `"[Number] [Category] skills / tools / patterns / habits that [outcome-with-quantified-benefit]"`. Examples:

The 7 that follow all pay above the average US tech salary."` — Inter Regular ~40 px white (note: **Regular weight, not Bold**, unlike the deep-dive paragraphs of images 51-54 — signals a "setup essay paragraph" rather than a "component definition"). 5 lines, ~40 words. Ends with a forward-tease (`"The 7 that follow…"`) — smart carousel-linkage.
3. **Nav chevrons** (y≈400) — flanking the paragraph mid-slide.
4. **NEW SUB-PRIMITIVE — Stat callout card** (`makeStatCalloutCard`) at y≈550–770, x≈45–1035, ~990 × 220 px:
   - **Container:** dark rounded rect (~radius 20 px), fill `~#1E1E1A` (very dark warm grey), **no border**. Subtle drop-shadow beneath.
   - **Big stat headline** (y≈580, ~x 90): `$112,521` in **yellow-highlight colour** `#E4C93C` (same `NEXTWORK_ACCENT_HIGHLIGHT` as image 56's inline pill), Inter Bold **~130 px** — huge, dominant.
   - **Explanatory sub-paragraph** (y≈650, ~x 90 → 970): `"is what the average US tech professional earns, and every role ahead beats it."` — Inter Bold ~34 px white, 2 lines. **The word `is` continues the sentence started by the stat** — the stat and the paragraph read as ONE sentence together (`"$112,521 is what the average US tech professional earns…"`).
   - **Citation line** (y≈735, ~x 90): `Dice 2025 Tech Salary Report` — Inter Regular ~24 px muted-grey `~#8A857B` — micro-attribution for the data point.

5. **Dot indicator** (y≈815) — 11 dots, dot 2 filled. Confirms slide 2.

**What's new / new primitives:**
- **`thesis-with-data-callout-card` bodyLayout** — universal for any argument-driven slide that pairs a claim (headline + paragraph) with a supporting data point (stat card). Applicable to any topic with a killer number (`$112k salary`, `90% failure rate`, `3x faster deploys`, `$1B saved`).
- **`makeStatCalloutCard` primitive** — 5/5 reusability. The big-yellow-number-on-dark-card pattern is a canonical stat-anchor.
- **The "stat continues into paragraph" typographic trick** (stat + `is what…`) is a nice writing pattern — LLM should generate stat + description as one grammatical sentence when possible.

**Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer):**
- **Thesis-headline formula:** `"[Subject] is not [common assumption]"` or `"[Subject] is actually [surprising claim]"` — counter-intuitive setup. 5-8 words.
- **Setup-paragraph formula:** 3-4 sentences, Regular weight (lighter than deep-dive body copy). Include a **forward-tease** at the end (`"The N that follow all…"`) to keep swipers engaged.
- **Stat-callout formula:** the stat should be a specific number with unit + a **grammatically continuing description** so the two elements read as one thought. `"$112,521 is what the average tech professional earns"` — perfect. Citation optional but boosts authority.

**Colour semantics extension:** the yellow-highlight colour `#E4C93C` established for inline text-highlight (image 56) is now also used for **stat callouts** in body slides. Confirms the yellow is nextwork's **"attention / money / free" semantic colour** — recommend renaming the token to `NEXTWORK_ACCENT_ATTENTION` for broader applicability.

**Reusability score:** 5/5 for the whole slide — thesis + paragraph + stat card is a universal argumentative-slide pattern for any data-driven carousel (SaaS metrics, salary reports, benchmark comparisons, market-size stats).

Slide 3 of 11, dot 3 filled. **This exact layout will repeat 6 more times** (once per skill), so it's the highest-ROI new template variant in the carousel. Introduces **3 new sub-primitives**.

**Layout order (top → bottom):**

1. **Progress-counter pill** (y≈75–130, x≈45–225, ~180 × 55 px): `skill 1 of 7` — small white rounded pill (fill `#F5F0E8` cream-white, radius = height/2), Inter Regular ~28 px near-black `#141414` text. Signals reader progression through the 7-skill list.

2. **Skill title** (y≈155–345): `Security architecture` — Inter Bold ~110 px white, 2 lines.

3. **Salary-range headline** (y≈380–475): `$138k to $176k` — Inter Bold ~90 px white, 1 line. Note the `k` shorthand — colloquial voice.

4. **Multiplier caption** (y≈500–540): `1.40x the average US tech salary` — Inter Regular ~30 px white. **Bridges** the range figure to the average (defined on the previous slide via the stat callout `$112,521`). Reader immediately grasps "this skill pays 40% above average".

5. **NEW PRIMITIVE — Compact bar chart** (`makeCompactBarChart`) at y≈555–670, x≈45–1035:
   - **2-row horizontal bar comparison.**
   - **Row 1: "This skill"**
     - Label at ~x 55: `This skill` in Inter Regular ~28 px white.
     - Bar at ~x 200 → 660: filled with **yellow-highlight** `#E4C93C`, ~30 px tall, rounded ends (radius = height/2).
     - Value at ~x 690: `$157,250` in Inter Bold ~30 px white.
   - **Row 2: "Tech average"**
     - Label at ~x 55: `Tech average` in Inter Regular ~28 px white.
     - Bar at ~x 200 → 470: filled with **muted-grey** `~#7A7A7A`, same ~30 px height. Shorter bar to visually convey "smaller number".
     - Value at ~x 690: `$112,521` in Inter Bold ~30 px white.
   - **Bar-length is proportional to the value** (`$157,250 / $200,000 ≈ 78%`, `$112,521 / $200,000 ≈ 56%` — visually calibrated to a max around ~$200k).
   - **Primitive signature:** `makeCompactBarChart({rows: Array<{label, value, valueFormatter, barColor, maxValue}>, barHeight, labelWidth, valueOffsetX, x, y, width})`. Reusable for any 2-3 row comparison chart (baseline vs new, before vs after, us vs them). Different from a full bar chart — this is a **compact inline** version for slides where the chart supports a paragraph, not the whole slide.

6. **Citation line** (y≈700–735, x≈45–830): `Robert Half 2026 Salary Guide, baseline Dice 2025 Tech Salary Report` — Inter Regular ~24 px muted-grey `#8A857B`. Attribution for both data points on the chart (this-skill number + tech-average number).

7. **NEW PRIMITIVE — Skill-tag pill row** (`makeSkillTagRow`) at y≈770–840, x≈45–740:
   - Horizontal row of 3 rounded-pill tags with ~12 px gaps.
   - **Tag 1: `Security Architect`** — filled dark-background pill (~`#141414` fill), Inter Bold ~28 px white text. This is the **primary/canonical job title** for this skill — filled treatment signals "this is what the role is called".
   - **Tag 2: `AWS`** — outlined pill (transparent fill, ~2 px white stroke), Inter Bold ~28 px white text. This is a **supporting skill tag** — outlined treatment signals "related keyword / stack tech".
   - **Tag 3: `Zero Trust`** — same outlined-pill treatment as `AWS`.
   - **Semantic contract:** first tag = **canonical job title** (filled/inverse); subsequent tags = **supporting keywords/tech** (outlined). Sets up a 2-tier hierarchy of tags in one visual row. Highly reusable pattern.
   - **Primitive signature:** `makeSkillTagRow({tags: Array<{label, variant: "filled" | "outlined"}>, gap, textColor, fillColor, strokeColor, radius, padding, x, y})`.

8. **Skill-description paragraph** (y≈870–1080, x≈45–1030, 4 lines): `"You design how a system defends itself before anyone writes code. That means deciding where the trust boundaries sit, so one stolen credential cannot reach everything."` — Inter Regular ~40 px white, ~34 words. Voice: 2nd-person, present tense (`You design`), plain-language ("stolen credential cannot reach everything"). Same "human-scale-consequence" writing style established for VPC carousel diagram captions.

9. **AWS icon tile** at bottom-left (y≈1100–1195, x≈50–145, ~95 × 95 px): small `aws` wordmark tile — signals the primary tool/platform for this skill. This is a **compact single-icon variant** of the AWS icon strip (image 48) — one icon at ~95 px instead of a row of 7 at 85 px. Recommend `makeAWSIconStrip({icons, size, layout: "row" | "single" | "grid"})` to accommodate this variant.

10. **Nav chevrons** (y≈400) — L+R, standard primitive.

11. **Dot indicator** (y≈1220) — 11 dots, dot 3 filled.

**Semantic contract of the whole slide:** each of the 7 skill deep-dives will follow this exact structure. LLM-writer contract: `{progressCounter: "skill N of 7", skillTitle, salaryRange, salaryMultiplier, chartData: {thisSkill: {label, value}, techAverage: {label, value}}, citation, tags: [primary, ...supporting], paragraph, primaryToolIcon}`.

**Reusability score:**
- **`skill-deep-dive-with-salary-chart` bodyLayout:** 5/5 — universal for any "N skills / roles / careers" carousel with salary data. Career-content is a huge Instagram niche.
- **`makeCompactBarChart` primitive:** 5/5 — reusable for any 2-3 row comparison metric across MANY carousel topics (perf benchmarks, revenue comparisons, before/after data).
- **`makeSkillTagRow` primitive with filled/outlined variant hierarchy:** 5/5 — reusable across any "primary label + supporting keywords" tag pattern (job titles, book categories, technology stacks, feature lists).
- **Progress-counter pill:** 5/5 — reusable on ANY carousel slide that's part of an N-of-M enumeration. Recommend `makeProgressCounterPill({current: number, total: number, label: string = "step" | "skill" | "part", style})`.

**Colour semantics extension:**
- **The yellow-highlight bar** `#E4C93C` is now used a THIRD time (image 56 inline pill, image 58 stat callout, and image 59 bar-chart bar) — confirmed as **the nextwork "attention / money / featured value" colour** for any element that represents "the number you should care about".
- **The muted grey bar** `#7A7A7A` for the "average" comparison confirms **grey = baseline / reference** in bar charts.

**Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer):**
- **Progress-counter formula:** `"skill N of M"` or `"step N of M"` or `"part N of M"` — lowercase, spelled-out numerals or Arabic (both work). Signals structured content.
- **Skill-title formula:** 1-3 word noun phrase, Title Case (`Security architecture`, `Identity & Access Management`, `Cloud posture management`). NOT job titles — the abstract skill area.
- **Salary-range formula:** `"$[low]k to $[high]k"` — Bold, direct, no fluff. Both numbers rounded to nearest 1k with `k` suffix.
- **Multiplier caption formula:** `"[N.NN]x the average [category] salary"` — computed from the midpoint of the salary range divided by the baseline stat. Include 2 decimal places.
- **Tag rules:** first tag = canonical job title (filled treatment), 2-3 supporting tags = tools/philosophies (outlined). Total 3-4 tags max.
- **Skill-paragraph formula:** 2 sentences, 2nd-person, present tense, ends with the **human-scale-consequence** (`"so one stolen credential cannot reach everything"`). ~30-40 words.

**Prediction (highly confident):** slides 60-64 will repeat this exact layout for skills 2-6, then slide 65 for skill 7. Slides 66-67 will be summary + CTA (echoing slides 55-56 of the VPC carousel).

   - **Attribution** below quote (y≈790, x≈65): `Dhatri, Founding Build Master` — Inter Bold ~22 px cream (semi-bold weight).
   - **Semantic:** person's own words framed as a design-noteworthy quote. Cream-on-orange colour treatment inverts the slide's palette to make the quote dominate.
   - **Primitive:** `makePullQuoteCard({quote, attribution, cardBgColor = "#B85A25", textColor = "#F5E8D0", quoteMarkColor, x, y, width, height})`.

7. **Nav chevrons L + R** — standard primitive.

8. **Dot indicator** (y≈760) — 3 dots, dot 2 filled. **Confirms 3-slide arc** and this is the middle slide.

**Layering (back → front):** `[cream canvas]` → `[name card]` → `[portrait card]` → `[rotated sticker tag with arrow-underline]` → `[4-facts grid]` → `[why-description card]` → `[pull-quote card]` → `[nav chevrons]` → `[dot indicator]`.

**Word count:** dense — ~120 words visible on the slide. But the density works because each element is spatially isolated and quickly scannable.

**Reusability score:**
- **`profile-with-facts-and-pullquote` bodyLayout:** 5/5 — the canonical **profile / team-member / featured-user** slide. Applicable to any brand: employees, community members, customer spotlights, podcast guests, artist features. 
- **`makeNameCard` chunky-cream primitive:** 5/5 — hero name treatment for any profile/spotlight slide.
- **`makeRotatedStickerTag` primitive:** 5/5 — playful editorial device, adds warmth to profile slides. Also reusable for "sale" / "new" / "featured" stickers in product carousels.
- **`makeFactsGrid` (4-column labelled facts):** 5/5 — universal for structured metadata (event details, product specs, member stats, project attributes).
- **`makeDescriptionCard` (Q&A cream-card):** 5/5 — reusable for any FAQ-style content block.
- **`makePullQuoteCard` (burnt-orange with big quote mark):** 5/5 — canonical pull-quote design; usable for any testimonial / featured-quote slide.

**Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer):**
- **Sticker tag formula:** short 2-4 word credentials with a `·` middle-dot separator (`design + informatics · sophomore`). Casual, italicized handwritten voice.
- **Facts-grid formula:** for each fact: `label` (2-3 words, sentence case, e.g. `On campus`) + `value` (2-5 words, could be a location, activity, time, or state). Icons chosen from a small library (people, heart, clock, video, star, pin, book).
- **Description-card formula:** header is a `"Why …?"` question, body is a 20-30 word direct answer.
- **Pull-quote formula:** direct-quote of 2 sentences, ~30-40 words, from the profile subject. Attribution: `[Name], [Role Title]`.

**Palette additions:**
- `NEXTWORK_SPOTLIGHT_CARD_CREAM ≈ #F0DFB8` — cream card fill (slightly darker than the canvas cream).
- `NEXTWORK_SPOTLIGHT_MUTED_TEXT ≈ #5A4A3A` — muted-brown for tiny labels.

**Notes / open questions:**
- **This is the DENSEST slide in the entire nextwork sample** — combines a portrait, name announcement, credential sticker, 4 facts, a description, AND a pull-quote in one canvas. Very high production value.
- **The mixture of chunky cream cards + burnt-orange accent card** creates a **2-tier visual hierarchy**: cream = content chrome (info-cards, name-card, facts-grid, description); orange = spotlight quote (the emotional anchor). Reader eye lands on the orange card last but hardest.
- **Predict slide 64** = the final CTA slide of the 3-slide arc — probably a "join Dhatri's group" or "meet more Build Masters" call-to-action with a URL pill (matching the VPC carousel's close-CTA pattern).
- **The `Caveat` handwritten font is reused** across the spotlight sub-brand (sticker tag) and the dark-cinematic sub-brand (handwritten annotations from images 53 + 55). Confirms **`Caveat` (or equivalent handwritten Google Font) is a globally-shipped nextwork font**, not just a per-template asset.

### Image 64 — `nextwork/image copy 16.png` (Cover of 3rd carousel: "If your resume was written by AI, 77% of recruiters know") — REDUCED DEPTH (cover variant + inline-highlight-pill on cover)

**Template classification:** `aurora-nextwork-cover` **variant** — 3rd distinct carousel cover in our sample. Same dark-cinematic family as images 48/57, but with new topic, new painterly artwork, and the **first cover-slide use of the inline-highlight-pill primitive** (previously only seen on the close-CTA slide 56).

**NEW CAROUSEL** — jumps out of the spotlight arc (which we thought had 3 slides but the 3rd is missing from our export). This is a new job-search / careers carousel.

**Cover deltas from images 48/57:**

1. **New painterly artwork:** cinematic dusk / cool-purple scene of a **long queue of dark-silhouetted people climbing a winding path** up a green hill toward a modern **glass office building** at the top. **Papers flutter in the foreground** (résumés being dropped/discarded). Purple-blue sky, olive-green hills, brick-orange path — cinematic and metaphor-rich (job-seekers climbing toward opportunity, papers as CVs). Excellent example of nextwork's painterly-artwork-as-topic-metaphor system.
   - New palette tokens for this artwork:
     - `NEXTWORK_ARTWORK_DUSK = ~#5A4A6B` — purple sky.
     - `NEXTWORK_ARTWORK_HILL = ~#4A6B4A` — olive hill.

2. **Headline with inline highlight pill** (y≈85–330, x≈50–590, ~540 × 245 px): `If your resume was written by AI, 77% of recruiters know` — Inter Bold ~90 px white, 3 lines. **The `77%` word is wrapped in a yellow-highlight pill** (same `makeInlineHighlightPill` primitive from image 56) — fill `#E4C93C`, radius ~14 px, thin dark stroke, text `77%` in Bold black. This is the **first time the inline pill appears on a cover slide** (previously only on close-CTA slide 56). Confirms: `makeInlineHighlightPill` works in BOTH cover headlines AND close-CTA headlines.
   - **New headline formula:** `"If [conditional clause], [stat-highlight] of [audience] [verb]"` — conditional-statistic-based headline. Very high-CTR archetype in career/marketing content. The stat in the pill is the **hook** — reader sees the huge yellow `77%` and can't look away.

3. **4-icon scattered arrangement** (y≈345–475, x≈65–430, ~365 × 130 px): **smaller-scale scatter** than image 57's 7-icon arrangement — only 4 job-search platforms:
   - **LinkedIn** — blue rounded-square tile with `in` glyph.
   - **Indeed** — white rounded-square tile with the lowercase `i` letter-mark.
   - **Workday** — white tile with the orange `W` mark.
   - **Google** — white tile with the multi-colour `G` mark (Google Jobs / Google Careers).
   - Each ~110 × 110 px, radius ~22 px, white fill with subtle shadow. Slight tilts (~2-5°) on some tiles giving a "sprinkled" feel.
   - **Semantic:** these are the **platforms recruiters use to filter/detect AI-written résumés**. Content-driven per carousel (same principle as image 48/57).
   - **Reuse `makeScatteredIconField` from image 57** with `icons: [linkedin, indeed, workday, google]`.

4. **Only right nav chevron** at mid-right (y≈400) — slide 1.

5. **Text-only brand marker at bottom-centre** (y≈755, centred): `NextWork` — Inter Bold ~30 px white text. Same primitive as image 57 (`text-only` variant of `makeBrandPill`).

6. **Dot indicator** at y≈755 (below the wordmark, ~y 785): **6 tiny white dots**, dot 1 filled. **Confirms 6-slide carousel** — a THIRD carousel length variant (11-slide long-form + 3-slide spotlight + 6-slide medium-form). Recommend supporting `carousel.length: 3 | 6 | 11` (or any length) as first-class.

**What's new / confirmed:**
- **3rd carousel-length variant (6 slides)** — nextwork uses multiple carousel lengths for different content types. Educational deep-dives are 11 slides; profile spotlights are 3 slides; **medium-format content (job-search / stat-driven career content) is 6 slides**.
- **Inline-highlight-pill in cover headline** — previously only on close-CTA. Now confirmed as a **any-headline primitive** for stat/keyword emphasis.
- **Cover-headline formula extension:** `"If [conditional], [highlighted stat]% of [audience] [verb-consequence]"` — hook-with-statistic formula, distinct from image 48's `"How does X actually work?"` and image 57's `"N skills that pay $X"`. Third headline archetype documented.

**Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer):**
- **Statistic-hook cover-headline formula:** `"If [thing you're doing], [X%] of [audience] [detect/notice/care about it]"` — conditional-statistic-conditional-consequence. Examples:
  - `"If your resume was written by AI, 77% of recruiters know"` (this slide)
  - `"If your code has no tests, 92% of engineers will avoid the repo"`
  - `"If you skip the intro, 60% of viewers drop off in 3 seconds"`

**Reusability score:** 4/5 for the cover (repeat pattern, no NEW primitives) — but the confirmation that inline-highlight-pill works on covers is a valuable finding.

**Notes / open questions:**
- **The queue-of-people-climbing painterly artwork** is one of the most metaphor-rich we've seen in nextwork. Each element in the scene doubles as a career metaphor: `queue` = competition, `hill` = effort, `glass building` = destination job, `fluttering papers` = discarded CVs. Recommend our LLM system prompt artwork with narrative-metaphor descriptions (`"applicants climbing toward opportunity, papers as CVs discarded along the path"`) rather than just topic keywords.
- **Predict slides 65-69** = the 5 body slides of this 6-slide carousel, likely covering AI-detection tools, resume writing tips, or interview prep techniques.
- **THIS IS THE 3RD DISTINCT CAROUSEL** in the nextwork sample: (1) VPC / 11 slides / warm painterly, (2) 7 security skills / 11 slides / cool painterly, (3) Resume-and-AI / 6 slides / dusk painterly. Confirms nextwork produces multiple carousel formats.

### nextwork/image copy 17.png — [REDUCED DEPTH — slide 65]
**Pattern class:** body slide of the 6-slide Resume/AI carousel (slide 2 of 6, dot 2 active).
**Overall vibe (1 line):** Stat-comparison body — headline `"LinkedIn takes 11,000 job applications a minute."` sits on the same dusk-queue painterly artwork as image 64, with a **before/after horizontal bar-comparison** anchoring the bottom half.

**Reuses (no new primitive):**
- Same dusk painterly artwork as image 64 (queue climbing hill toward glass building).
- Left + right white chevrons (mid-card), same primitive.
- `NextWork` text wordmark bottom-centre + 6-dot indicator (dot 2 filled).
- Source-attribution line at bottom-left in small white 22 px Regular: `LinkedIn, reported by The New York Times, June 2025` — same primitive as image 58's source-line, but this is the **first time we see it on a nextwork body slide** (previously seen on the stat-callout card). **Confirms:** source-attribution line is a nextwork body-slide staple whenever a stat is cited.

**NEW BODY LAYOUT — `bodyLayout: "stat-comparison-bar-race"`:**
- Headline top-left (Inter Bold ~72 px white, 2 lines, big stat `11,000` inline within the sentence — NOT wrapped in a pill this time, just white Bold like the rest of the headline).
- Below the headline: **branded-source icon tile** (LinkedIn blue rounded-square with `in` glyph, ~90 × 90 px, radius ~18 px) — anchors the visual as being "about LinkedIn".
- Two stacked comparison rows below the icon:
  - Row 1 label: `a year earlier   7,600 a minute` (white Bold ~28 px), then a **cream-white horizontal bar** below (~380 × 42 px, radius ~10 px, fill `~#F0EAD8`) representing the smaller/earlier stat.
  - Row 2 label: `now   11,000 a minute` (Bold ~28 px, `now` in yellow `#E4C93C`, rest in white), then a **yellow bar** (~460 × 42 px, radius ~10 px, fill `#E4C93C`) that is visibly LONGER than row 1's bar — the length ratio (~460/380 ≈ 1.21) roughly matches the value ratio (11,000/7,600 ≈ 1.45), so **bar lengths are proportional to the values, not painted 1:1** — this is a compressed / stylised bar chart, not a precise chart.
  - Yellow-filled bar has a subtle mint-green stroke `#4AC48D` (~2 px) — first time a nextwork stat-bar has a coloured stroke. Adds "highlight-worthy" energy.
  - Category label above each bar (not axis-attached, floating labels).
- **Semantic:** this is a **bar-race / before-vs-now visual** — reader instantly perceives "growth" from bar lengths without reading the numbers.

**New Fabric.js primitive (add to library):**
- `makeStatComparisonBars({ rows: [{ label, value, bar: { color, strokeColor?, widthRatio } }], activeIndex, y, x, width })` — renders N stacked labelled bars where bar widths are proportional to their `widthRatio` (0–1), with the "active/highlight" row using an accent stroke. Composed of a repeated `label-text + bar-rectangle` primitive stack.
- Base primitive: `makeStatBarRow({ label, valueText, barColor, barStrokeColor?, widthPx, labelAccentWord? })` — one row = a label line (with optional accent-coloured word like `now` in yellow) + a filled horizontal bar underneath.

**New palette tokens:**
- `NEXTWORK_STATBAR_CREAM = #F0EAD8` — inactive/reference bar fill.
- Confirms `NEXTWORK_ACCENT_HIGHLIGHT = #E4C93C` doubles as the "active bar fill" colour (already locked from image 56/64).

**Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer):**
- **Stat-comparison-bar headline formula:** `"[Platform/Entity] [verb] [current-stat] [unit]"` — big absolute number, single-sentence, factual + present-tense. Examples:
  - `"LinkedIn takes 11,000 job applications a minute"` (this slide)
  - `"YouTube gets 500 hours of video uploaded a minute"`
- Comparison-row labels are terse: `[time-frame]   [value] [unit]` — 2-part, tab-separated. Time-frame in front, value in back with the accent colour on the time-frame word (`now`) rather than the number.
- Always cite the source in a tiny bottom-left line — nextwork treats stat-citing as non-negotiable brand hygiene.

**Reusability score:** 5/5 — introduces a genuinely NEW bodyLayout (bar-race comparison) that generalises to any before/after growth-stat slide.

**Notes / open questions:**
- The **bar length ratio is stylised (visually inflated)** — actual 11k/7.6k = 1.45× but rendered ~1.21×. Suggests nextwork prioritises visual harmony over strict data accuracy. Our LLM writer should compute both the true ratio and a "capped-max-1.5×" visual ratio to keep bars readable.
- The `now` word in yellow is a subtle but effective device: **the eye lands on `now` first**, then reads the current number. Recommend our stat-bar helper always highlight the active timeframe word (not the number), matching this pattern.

### nextwork/image copy 18.png — [REDUCED DEPTH — slide 66]
**Pattern class:** body slide of Resume/AI carousel — "myth vs reality" essay slide with product-mockup hero.
**Overall vibe (1 line):** Headline `"AI just helps you get past the resume filter."` sits at the bottom over the dusk-queue painterly bg, with a **fake browser-mockup of Rezi.ai (an AI resume builder)** and a **4-tile scattered-icon row** of AI tools filling the top half, plus a **new "framing-label pill"** primitive above the headline.

**Carousel-length correction:** dot indicator here shows **~9 dots with dot 3 filled** — NOT the 6-dot layout I predicted after image 64. Either (a) image 64's dot count was slightly off (very possible — small dots on a dark bg), or (b) this is a **different** longer carousel using the same dusk-queue artwork. Recommend logging the Resume/AI carousel as **9 slides, not 6**. Update the earlier note.

**Reuses (no NEW primitives, but heavy composition):**
- Dusk-queue painterly artwork (same as images 64, 65).
- `makeBrowserWindowMockup` primitive (originally from image 56) — this time rendering **Rezi.ai's real product page** with macOS traffic lights (red/yellow/green), a URL-pill in title bar showing `Rezi`, a real-looking headline `Free AI Resume Builder. / Build, improve, & score / your resume`, right-side testimonial block (`Trusted by over 4 million job seekers and backed by Forbes as the best AI resume builder`), a blue `Try for free` button + `4.7/5.0` rating, and CTA row `Upload or drop your resume to get started` / `Score resume`. Confirms `makeBrowserWindowMockup` works as a canonical "screenshot of a competitor / referenced product" primitive. This is nextwork's second use.
- `makeScatteredIconField` (from image 57) — this time with 4 AI-tool tiles arranged in a **compact horizontal-row** (not a diamond scatter) beneath the browser mockup: **OpenAI/ChatGPT** logo (black circular knot on white tile), **Claude** (orange sunburst/asterisk on white), **Gemini** (blue 4-point star on white), **Copilot** (teal `C` on white). Each ~90 × 90 px, radius ~20 px. **Confirms `makeScatteredIconField` supports a `layout: "row"` variant** in addition to `"diamond"` / `"triangle"` / `"custom"`.
- L + R chevrons; dot indicator (9 dots, dot 3 active).

**NEW SUB-PRIMITIVE — Framing-label pill (`makeFramingLabelPill`):**
- Above the headline (y≈460–510, x≈50–360, ~310 × 55 px): a **cream-white rounded pill** (`~#F5F0E8`, `NEXTWORK_ACCENT_CREAM`), radius = height/2 (~28 px), containing the text `what most people believe` in Inter Bold ~26 px near-black `#141414`.
- **Semantic role:** labels the sentence below it as **the common misconception** the slide is about to refute. Sets up a **myth-vs-reality rhetorical structure** — the next slide (slide 4 of this carousel, image 67) will almost certainly have a paired label `what actually happens` or `here's the truth` with the refuting statement below.
- Distinguished from `makeInlineHighlightPill` (image 56/64) by: (a) sits **above** the sentence as a standalone element, not inline within it; (b) uses cream fill not yellow; (c) is a **whole-sentence label** not a single-word highlight.
- **Primitive signature:** `makeFramingLabelPill({ text, x, y, fillColor = "#F5F0E8", textColor = "#141414", fontSize = 26, padding, radius })`. Highly reusable — every "myth vs reality", "before / after", "problem / solution", "old way / new way" carousel gets these paired labels.

**Headline** (y≈525–730, x≈50–620, ~570 × 205 px): `AI just helps you get past the resume filter.` — Inter Bold ~72 px white, 2 lines. Note the trailing **period** (not a question mark) — a **declarative myth statement**. Voice: neutral, reportorial, "here's what most people say" — sets up the refutation on the next slide.

**Layering (back → front):**
`[dusk artwork]` → `[browser mockup top-half card + traffic lights + URL pill + product screenshot content]` → `[4-icon scattered-row of AI tools]` → `[cream framing-label pill]` → `[headline]` → `[L + R chevrons]` → `[dot indicator]`.

**Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer):**
- **Myth-vs-reality carousel structure:** paired slides where slide N carries `[framing: "what most people believe"] + [myth statement]` and slide N+1 carries `[framing: "what actually happens"] + [refutation statement]`. Nextwork may repeat this pair 2-3 times in a 9-slide carousel to cover multiple myths.
- **Framing-label formula:** 3-6 word lowercase description of the rhetorical position, e.g. `what most people believe`, `here's the truth`, `what recruiters actually do`, `what changes in 2025`. Cream pill background = "setup / context"; contrast to yellow inline pill = "attention / hook".
- **Myth-statement formula:** short declarative, present tense, uses a **downplaying adverb** like `just`, `only`, `merely`, `simply`, or `basically` (`AI just helps you get past the filter`, `Recruiters only care about keywords`, `A resume is basically a formality`). The downplaying adverb sets up the refutation on the next slide, which will demolish the "just / only" framing.

**Cross-slide narrative device:** confirms **paired-slide rhetoric** as a nextwork body-slide technique — a single narrative arc spans slides N and N+1 (framing + refutation). This is different from the standalone-slide model of the VPC carousel. Recommend our system prompt LLMs to plan **paired slides** as a first-class unit when appropriate.

**Reusability score:** 5/5 — even though the individual primitives are reuses, the **combination of `browser-mockup + scattered-icon-row + framing-label-pill + declarative-myth-headline`** is a clean, reusable **myth-slide bodyLayout**. Recommend `bodyLayout: "myth-with-product-evidence"`.

**Notes / open questions:**
- **The Rezi.ai mockup is a real competitor product** — nextwork is doing a **product callout** to establish "here's what these AI resume builders promise". Confirms nextwork's editorial voice can reference real 3rd-party products in body slides (not just their own product as on the close-CTA).
- **Predict slide 67** (image copy 19.png) = paired-slide refutation. Will likely have a matching cream framing pill saying something like `what really happens` or `the truth`, plus a contrasting statement demolishing the "AI just helps you get past the filter" myth. Watch for this exact pairing.
- **Dot indicator = 9, not 6.** Update prior notes: the Resume/AI carousel is at least 9 slides long, not 6. If image 64's dot count was wrong, we may also need to re-check the exact carousel-length variants. Confirmed: nextwork uses **variable-length carousels** (3 / 9 / 11 slides observed), not a fixed 3-tier system.

Confirms `makeBrowserWindowMockup` supports arbitrary `contentEmbed` groups — this one has an embedded gallery of card thumbnails.

Useful for any "browse gallery" mockup (design tools, template stores, portfolio grids). Not a critical primitive, but nice-to-have.

Each ~110 × 110 px, radius ~22 px. Same "sprinkled with slight tilts" energy as image 64.

**Headline** (y≈530–770, x≈50–620, ~570 × 240 px): `77% of hiring managers think many resumes are now written by AI.` — Inter Bold ~80 px white, 3 lines. **The `77%` stat is NOT wrapped in an inline highlight pill** this time — it just leads the sentence in the same white Bold as everything else. Different from image 64 where `77%` WAS pill-wrapped. **Design contract:** the inline-highlight pill is used when the stat is the surprise / hook (cover slide, e.g. image 64); when the reader has already been primed for the stat (this is the payoff slide of the pair, not the setup), plain Bold is enough. LLM should decide pill-vs-plain based on **narrative role** (surprise-first-appearance = pill; refutation / payoff / recall = plain Bold).

**NEW SUB-PRIMITIVE — Citation with context-label (`makeCitationWithContextLine`):**
- Two-line citation block at bottom-left (y≈865–950, x≈50–500):
  - Line 1: `Resume Genius, 2026 Hiring Insights Report.` — Inter Regular ~26 px white — the actual source citation.
  - Line 2: `Template gallery shown: Novorésumé` — Inter Regular ~26 px white — **contextual note about the mockup shown**, distinct from the stat citation.
- **Semantic:** nextwork separates **the-stat-citation** from **the-mockup-attribution** on TWO adjacent lines. Signals meticulousness: reader knows both where the number came from AND what product is being pictured. Recommend our LLM writer emit these as separate fields: `stat.citation` + `mockup.attribution`, rendered as a 2-line block.
- **Primitive signature:** `makeCitationWithContextLine({ citation: string, contextLine?: string, textColor, fontSize, x, y })`.

**Layering (back → front):**
`[dusk artwork]` → `[browser mockup with template-gallery content, mint CTA button, Powered-by-AI pill]` → `[3-icon row of tool tiles]` → `[headline]` → `[citation + context lines]` → `[chevrons]` → `[dot indicator]`.

**Colour semantics confirmed:**
- The **mint-green `#4AC48D`** (`NEXTWORK_ACCENT_MINT`) is used a 3rd time on this slide (Powered-by-AI pill in the mockup + Explore CTA button) — confirming mint is not just an "internet-gateway topic colour" but nextwork's **general "positive / go / featured-CTA" accent**.
- The `Powered by AI` pill in the mockup is a mini `makeInlineHighlightPill` variant with mint fill instead of yellow — could unify as `makeInlinePill({ text, fillColor, textColor, style: "highlight" | "tag" | "featured" })`.

**Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer):**
- **Statistic-headline formula (payoff-slide variant):** big absolute stat followed by a qualitative claim, present tense, ends with a period. `"[X%] of [audience] think / know / see that [claim]"`. Examples:
  - `"77% of hiring managers think many resumes are now written by AI."` (this slide)
  - `"64% of engineers say tests catch more bugs than code review."`
  - `"91% of readers skip the intro if it's over 3 sentences."`
- **Citation-with-context formula:** always two lines when a mockup is shown — `[Source Name, Year Report Title].` + `[Mockup / product shown: Name]`. Never merge into one line.
- **Confirmed pill-vs-plain rule:** inline highlight pill for **surprise-first-appearance** headlines (cover / hook); plain Bold for **payoff / recall / refutation** headlines.

**Reusability score:** 4/5 — no new primitives, but confirms the mockup + icon-row + stat-headline + citation pattern as a repeatable **stat-hook body layout** (`bodyLayout: "stat-hook-with-product-mockup"`). Compose with existing helpers.

**Notes / open questions:**
- **Paired-slide pattern DEBUNKED / refined:** I predicted image 67 would be a matching `what really happens` refutation to image 66's `what most people believe`. Instead, this slide **doubles down on the hook stat** (77% of managers think resumes are AI-written). So the carousel structure may not always be strict A/B myth-vs-reality pairs — instead nextwork sometimes uses **sequences of related stat-slides** to build a case before pivoting. Recommend our LLM planner treat myth-refutation and stat-piling as two distinct multi-slide arcs.
- **The `77%` stat appears on BOTH the cover (image 64, with pill) AND slide 4 (this slide, without pill).** Confirmed: **recurring-stat callback** across a carousel is a nextwork narrative technique — plant a stat on the cover, echo it later without the visual emphasis. LLM writer should support `carousel.echoedStats: Array<{ stat, appearsOn: [slideIndexes] }>` to plan intentional recall.

### nextwork/image copy 20.png — [REDUCED DEPTH — slide 68]
**Pattern class:** body slide of Resume/AI carousel — stat-headline with **iconographic-metaphor + LinkedIn help-doc mockup**. Dot indicator: 9 dots, dot 5 filled. Slide 5 of 9.
**Overall vibe (1 line):** Payoff-stat headline `"Gartner expects 1 in 4 candidate profiles to be fake by 2028."` anchored bottom, with a **row of 4 flat-vector figure-icons (3 cream + 1 yellow labelled `fake`)** at the top and a **LinkedIn Help-page mockup** for the "Get verified" flow in the middle. First slide in nextwork sample to use **flat-vector iconography as a hero-visual data metaphor**.

**Reuses:**
- Dusk-queue painterly bg (5th slide with same artwork).
- L + R chevrons; dot indicator (9 dots, dot 5 active).
- `makeBrowserWindowMockup` variant used to render the **LinkedIn Help / Verifications** page — traffic lights implied but not visible in this crop; instead there's a LinkedIn `in` icon + `Help` header + `LinkedIn ▼ | How can we help?` search input row, then a `Verifications on your LinkedIn profile` heading + "2 months ago" timestamp + a `Get verified` card with grey body-copy paragraph and a blue `Verify now` CTA button (real LinkedIn blue `~#0A66C2`). Confirms the browser-mockup primitive can render **help-doc / support-page UIs** in addition to product-landing pages.
- Citation-with-context line at bottom-left (**refined pattern from slide 67**): `Gartner, 2025.  A fake profile is someone posing as another person, or having someone else sit the interview.` — Inter Regular ~26 px white, 2 lines. **This slide adds a NEW variant of the context-line: it's not a "mockup shown" note but a DEFINITION-CONTEXT note** — clarifies what "fake profile" means so the stat lands with legal precision. Update the primitive to support `contextType: "mockup-attribution" | "definition" | "methodology"`.

**NEW SUB-PRIMITIVE — Flat-vector figure-icon row with a highlighted anomaly (`makeFigureRowWithAnomaly`):**
- At the top of the slide (y≈80–290, x≈70–420, ~350 × 210 px): a **row of 4 flat-vector stick-figure/person silhouettes** — the classic "1 out of N" visual metaphor.
  - 3 figures on the left: **cream/warm-white fill** `~#F0EAD8` (`NEXTWORK_STATBAR_CREAM`), matte flat vector (rounded head circle + trapezoidal body). All identical.
  - 4th figure on the right: **yellow fill** `#E4C93C` (`NEXTWORK_ACCENT_HIGHLIGHT`), same silhouette shape. Sits at a slightly rotated angle (~5° tilt right) — visually calls out that it's the "different one" from the row.
  - Small label BELOW the yellow figure: `fake` — Inter Regular ~26 px, colour matches the yellow figure `#E4C93C`. **A word-label directly beneath the anomaly** visually connects the label to the stat.
- **Semantic:** the **"1-in-N with 1 highlighted"** iconographic metaphor is one of the most **canonical data-viz patterns** for infographics ("1 in 4 people", "1 in 5 kids", "N out of M events"). Nextwork's version is stylised — flat silhouettes, no shading, one accent colour for the anomaly.
- **Primitive signature:** `makeFigureRowWithAnomaly({ totalFigures: number, anomalyIndex: number, defaultColor, anomalyColor, anomalyLabel?, figureShape: "person" | "square" | "circle" | "custom-svg", size, gap, tilt?: {angle, target: "anomaly" | "all"} })`. Reusable for any "1 in N" stat visualization — job applications, disease incidence, product defects, demographic ratios, survey responses.
- **Reusability:** 5/5 — every stat-driven carousel with a ratio benefits from this primitive. Universal infographic device.

**Headline** (y≈530–740, x≈50–650, ~600 × 210 px): `Gartner expects 1 in 4 candidate profiles to be fake by 2028.` — Inter Bold ~76 px white, 3 lines. **The stat `1 in 4` is NOT wrapped in a pill** — it's rendered as plain Bold text inline within the sentence, consistent with the "payoff-stat = plain Bold" rule discovered on slide 67.

**Voice / attribution pattern:** starts with the source-authority verb-attribution (`Gartner expects [stat]`), NOT the stat itself. Different from slide 67's `77% of hiring managers think…`. **Attribution-first headline formula** — `"[Authority] [expects/says/reports/finds] [stat] [context]"` — is another payoff-headline archetype. LLM writer should support both patterns: **stat-first** (`77% of…`) or **authority-first** (`Gartner expects…`). Authority-first is more journalistic; stat-first is more punchy/social.

**Layering (back → front):**
`[dusk painterly artwork]` → `[flat-vector figure row + `fake` label]` → `[LinkedIn Help mockup card]` → `[headline]` → `[citation + definition line]` → `[L + R chevrons]` → `[dot indicator]`.

**Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer):**
- **Authority-first headline formula:** `"[Reputable Source] [predicts / expects / says / finds] [stat] [subject noun] to be [descriptor] by [year / condition]."` — starts with a big-name analyst / research / news source. Examples:
  - `"Gartner expects 1 in 4 candidate profiles to be fake by 2028."` (this slide)
  - `"Meta says 30% of engineering hires now come from referrals."`
  - `"McKinsey finds 65% of executives feel unprepared for GenAI."`
- **Figure-row-anomaly formula:** always use "1 in N" not "X%" when the stat is a **small integer ratio** (1 in 4, 1 in 5, 2 in 10). The visual metaphor works best with 4-10 figures total. Never use for large denominators (1 in 100 doesn't work as icons).
- **Context-line variants:** `contextType: "mockup-attribution"` (slide 67, `Template gallery shown: X`) vs `contextType: "definition"` (this slide, `A fake profile is someone posing as another person…`) vs `contextType: "methodology"` (potential future, `Based on surveys of 5,000+ recruiters`). All three sit as a second line under the primary citation.

**Notes / open questions:**
- **The LinkedIn Help mockup being shown here** signals a **cross-slide narrative arc**: slide 68 states the problem (1 in 4 are fake), slide 69 (image copy 21.png, my next read) likely shows the solution flow. Predict slide 69 will be a "here's how to get LinkedIn Verified" walkthrough — either a step-list or a screenshot of the actual Verify-Now flow.
- **The tilt on the yellow figure** (~5° rotation) is a small but effective humanising touch — signals "this one doesn't fit the mold". Recommend our LLM writer suggest `tilt: 5` on anomaly figures by default (with optional `tilt: 0` for stricter data-viz voice).
- **Confirmed:** nextwork's `NEXTWORK_ACCENT_HIGHLIGHT` `#E4C93C` yellow now serves FOUR semantic roles: (1) inline text-highlight pill (slide 56), (2) stat-callout card colour (slide 58), (3) active bar-chart bar (slides 59-61, 65), (4) infographic anomaly figure (this slide). Solidly locked as the "attention / money / featured / anomaly" all-purpose yellow.

### nextwork/image copy 21.png — [REDUCED DEPTH — slide 69]
**Pattern class:** body slide of Resume/AI carousel — **company-logo-row + progress-bar stat visualization**. Dot indicator: 9 dots, dot 8 filled (slide 8 of 9, penultimate).
**Overall vibe (1 line):** Headline top `"Google, Cisco and McKinsey brought back the onsite interview."` + **white card containing 3 company logos side-by-side** + **percent-filled progress bar** with caption `"72% of recruiting leaders now interview in person"` — a new "authority-companies + progress-bar-stat" body layout.

**Reuses:**
- Dusk-queue painterly bg (6th slide in a row — heavily locked as this carousel's ambient artwork).
- L + R chevrons; dot indicator (9 dots, dot 8 active).
- Citation-with-context line at bottom-left: `Gartner survey of recruiting leaders, Computerworld 2025` — same `makeCitationWithContextLine` primitive from slide 67/68. This one is a **single-line citation** (no context/definition line) — confirms the context-line is optional.
- Text-only `NextWork` wordmark (implicit).

**Two NEW SUB-PRIMITIVES on this slide:**

**1. Company-logo card row (`makeCompanyLogoCardRow`):**
- Y≈355–475, x≈50–1030, ~980 × 120 px: a single **wide white rounded-rect card** (fill `#FFFFFF`, radius ~24 px, subtle drop shadow) containing **3 real company logos side-by-side**:
  - **Google** (~x 150, ~y 400): the multi-colour `G` mark (blue/red/yellow/green) at ~90 × 90 px. Real Google brand logo.
  - **Cisco** (~x 500, ~y 400): the classic Cisco wordmark with the blue vertical-bars logo above the text. ~180 × 60 px. Real Cisco brand logo.
  - **McKinsey & Company** (~x 830, ~y 400): the McKinsey navy-blue rectangular badge with white text `McKinsey&Company`. Real McKinsey logo.
- Logos are **evenly distributed with generous whitespace** — the card acts as a "credibility-anchor" visual, listing the well-known companies that back the claim in the headline.
- **Primitive signature:** `makeCompanyLogoCardRow({ logos: Array<{name: string, logoUrl: string, size: number}>, cardBgColor = "#FFFFFF", cardRadius, drop-shadow, x, y, width, height, gap })`. Reusable for any "companies that agree" / "customers that use us" / "tech stack in production" visual. LinkedIn, product-marketing, and career carousels use this pattern constantly.
- **Reusability:** 5/5 — universal social-proof primitive.

**2. Percent-filled progress-bar with caption (`makeProgressBarStat`):**
- Y≈580–660, x≈50–1030 (~980 × 55 px): a **single horizontal capsule bar** with a filled proportion + surrounding empty portion.
  - Full-width bar container: rounded rectangle, ~980 × 45 px, radius ~22 px, fill = grey `~#7A7A7A` (the "empty / remainder" portion).
  - **Filled portion**: from x=50 to x≈755 (~705 px wide, ~72% of the bar's width), rounded-rect capsule shape overlaid on the container, fill = **yellow-highlight** `#E4C93C` (`NEXTWORK_ACCENT_HIGHLIGHT`). The filled portion is proportional to the stat value (`72%` → 72% of the bar width).
  - **Caption directly ABOVE the bar** (y≈540, x≈50, ~980 × 40 px): `72% of recruiting leaders now interview in person` — Inter Bold ~34 px, colour is **the same yellow `#E4C93C`** as the bar (label + bar match in colour, tying the two elements together). 1 line.
- **Distinct from `makeCompactBarChart` (image 59):** compact-bar-chart has multiple rows with per-row labels/values; this is a **single bar** representing ONE stat, with the caption tightly bound above. Semantically like a horizontal-gauge / percent-fill indicator.
- **Primitive signature:** `makeProgressBarStat({ label: string, percent: number (0-100), fillColor, remainderColor, labelColor?, cardHeight, cornerRadius, x, y, width })`. Reusable for any single-stat progress-bar / gauge visual. Applicable to conversion rates, adoption percentages, exam pass rates, completion rates, etc.
- **Reusability:** 5/5 — clean, minimal single-stat visual.

**Headline** (y≈70–260, x≈50–1030, ~980 × 190 px): `Google, Cisco and McKinsey brought back the onsite interview.` — Inter Bold ~72 px white, 3 lines (`Google, Cisco and` / `McKinsey brought back` / `the onsite interview.`). **Company names lead the sentence** — classic authority-first headline archetype. Ends with a period.

**Layering (back → front):**
`[dusk artwork]` → `[headline]` → `[white company-logo card + 3 logos]` → `[percent-fill progress-bar with yellow caption]` → `[chevrons]` → `[citation line]` → `[dot indicator]`.

**Composition semantics:** the slide layers **THREE separate credibility signals**:
1. **Headline claim** (`Google, Cisco, McKinsey brought back onsite interviews`).
2. **Visual proof** (their actual logos on a clean white card — reader can't dispute this).
3. **Quantified stat with progress bar** (`72% of recruiting leaders now interview in person`).
Each layer independently reinforces the claim. Reader has three ways to be convinced. Excellent example of **multi-modal-evidence stacking** on a single slide — recommend our system model `slide.evidenceLayers` for stat-driven claims.

**Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer):**
- **Named-authority-list headline formula:** `"[Company A], [Company B] and [Company C] [past-tense verb] [contrarian action]"` — lists 3-4 recognisable companies + a surprising action they've taken. Examples:
  - `"Google, Cisco and McKinsey brought back the onsite interview."` (this slide)
  - `"Stripe, Netflix and Uber all use written work-samples in tech hiring."`
  - `"Amazon, Meta and Apple killed the take-home coding challenge."`
  - Voice: journalistic-authoritative, past tense (`brought back`, `killed`, `switched to`), 3 companies is the magic number (2 = weak, 4+ = list starts to lose punch).
- **Progress-bar caption formula:** `"[X%] of [audience] [verb] [action / behaviour]"` — Inter Bold, coloured to match the bar. Examples:
  - `"72% of recruiting leaders now interview in person"` (this slide)
  - `"58% of engineers still write commit messages manually"`
- **Rule:** the label caption should be **actionable / behaviour-based**, not descriptive. `72% now interview in person` (action) beats `72% is high` (adjective).

**Notes / open questions:**
- **The white-card-with-3-logos is a signature social-proof pattern.** Nextwork does it clean — no case-study text, no "trusted by" caption above the card. Just the logos and a headline that names them. Recommend our LLM writer treat the logos as **self-evident authority** (no meta-copy needed).
- **The bar-and-caption yellow-matching** is a strong visual-alignment device. Label and bar are the same yellow — reader's eye subconsciously connects "the caption describes what the bar represents". Recommend the primitive ALWAYS use matching colour for `labelColor` and `fillColor` unless explicitly overridden.
- **This is likely the "here's the counter-evidence" slide of the Resume/AI carousel** — slides 66-68 built up "AI resumes are everywhere / 77% suspect / 1 in 4 profiles fake" and this slide 69 shifts to "and here's how top companies are responding: they've brought back onsite interviews". Narrative arc: problem → problem → problem → response. Confirms **nextwork body slides use ~4-slide problem-response arcs**, not just A/B pairs.
- **Slide 70 (image copy 22.png, my next read)** predicted to be the FINAL close-CTA slide of this 9-slide carousel — probably a "how to prep for onsite interviews" or "learn interview skills at nextwork.ai" CTA. Watch for the URL pill.
- **The 3 logos here are commercial trademarks** — nextwork uses them without any "used with permission" caveat. Confirms nextwork treats named-company references as fair-use editorial commentary. Our brand-safety guardrail: LLM should stay in this same "editorial reference" mode (naming, not endorsing / partnering / claiming affiliation).

Distinct from slide 55's earlier close-CTA which used a single-bullet + browser mockup — this variant is **pure-text recap + full-width brand-CTA card at bottom**, no product mockup.

**Layout zones (portrait 648 × 810):**
- **Section label** (y≈180, x≈75, ~180 × 34 px): `The takeaway` — yellow `#E4C93C` Inter Bold ~28 px. **NEW label style** — coloured section-header (not a pill, not a plain white label). Signals "here's the summary" mode. Recommend token `NEXTWORK_SECTION_LABEL_YELLOW` reuses `NEXTWORK_ACCENT_HIGHLIGHT`.
- **Bulleted recap list** (y≈230–490, x≈75–590, ~515 × 260 px): 3 bullets, each ~2 lines Inter Bold ~28 px white, with yellow-dot bullets (`•` in `#E4C93C`, ~10 px). Line-height loose (~1.35). Bullet 1: `AI gets your application seen, and it also gets it doubted.` — problem-recap. Bullet 2: `Employers moved the real test into the room.` — market-response-recap. Bullet 3: `What wins now is being able to explain your own work out loud.` — actionable-lesson.
- **Chevrons** (y≈390, x≈35 & 585, both 44 × 44 px): standard grey-transparent circles.
- **Brand-CTA card** (y≈620–735, x≈40–605, ~565 × 115 px, cream `#F0EAD8` fill, corner radius ~18 px): full-width cream card with:
  - **Left icon** (x≈75, y≈650, ~50 × 50 px): NextWork globe/lattice logo (thin-line black circle with meridian + latitude lines — the brand icon-mark).
  - **Two-line black text** (Inter Bold ~22 px): line 1 `Both sides of the table now run on AI.` / line 2 `Getting good at it takes real projects.`
  - **Third line with inline yellow pill** (y≈705, ~22 px): `NextWork gives you those` + inline yellow pill `for free` (`#E4C93C` fill, black text, ~90 × 32 px, corner ~8 px).
- **Dot indicator** (y≈762, 9 dots centered ~x=224–424): 9th dot filled white, first 8 grey — confirms 9-slide carousel length.

**NEW primitives / tokens (flag for build):**
- `makeSectionLabelText({ label, color, x, y, fontSize })` — simple coloured section-header, no background. Distinct from `makeBrandPill` / `makeFramingLabelPill` (both have fills). Reusable across all "The takeaway" / "The lesson" / "In summary" section markers.
- `makeBulletedRecapList({ bullets: string[], bulletColor, textColor, bulletSize, lineHeight, x, y, width })` — vertically-stacked bullets with coloured-dot marker. NEW primitive — none of slides 48-69 used this exact bullet-list pattern (concept-contrast used 2-column, numbered-list used flow-verbs with numbers). Reusable for any recap / summary / rules-of-thumb slide.
- `makeBrandCTACard({ icon, primaryLines: string[], ctaSuffix, pillLabel, pillColor, cardFillColor, textColor, x, y, width, cornerRadius })` — the cream card with globe-mark + 2-line pitch + inline-pill CTA. This is the **canonical nextwork close-CTA card format** (distinct from slide 55's browser-mockup CTA). Sub-primitive: `makeNextworkGlobeIcon({ x, y, size, strokeColor })` — thin-line globe/lattice mark, ~1 px stroke, purely vector, reusable as brand-avatar.
- **NEW palette confirmation:** `NEXTWORK_CTA_CARD_CREAM = #F0EAD8` — same as `NEXTWORK_STATBAR_CREAM` from slide 65. **Consolidation:** these are the same token. Rename globally to `NEXTWORK_CREAM_NEUTRAL` and reuse for stat-bar-inactive + CTA-card fills. Single-source-of-truth colour reduces palette sprawl.

**Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer) — "The takeaway" recap formula:**
- **Section-label:** always 2 words, definite article + noun (`The takeaway` / `The lesson` / `The rule`). Yellow Inter Bold.
- **3-bullet recap structure** (mirrors problem-response-arc):
  1. **Problem-recap** — restate the tension in 1 sentence (`AI gets your app seen AND doubted`).
  2. **Market-response-recap** — restate what the market did in response (`Employers moved the real test into the room`).
  3. **Actionable-lesson** — the reader's takeaway, always an *ability* not a task (`What wins now is being able to explain your own work out loud`). Voice: aphoristic, present-tense, ends with a verb-phrase.
- **Rule:** exactly 3 bullets. 2 = incomplete arc, 4+ = feels like a listicle re-run.
- **Brand-CTA line structure:** line 1 = market-diagnosis (`Both sides of the table now run on AI`), line 2 = solution-hook (`Getting good at it takes real projects`), line 3 = brand-offer with inline-pill on the noun (`NextWork gives you those` + pill `for free`). The pill lands on the **value-signal word** (`for free`, `today`, `now`), not the brand name.

**Notes:**
- **Carousel-length confirmed: 9 slides** — Resume/AI carousel is officially 9 slides (cover + 7 body + close-CTA). Add to `template.carouselLengths.resume-ai: 9` alongside `vpc: 11` and `security-skills: 11`.
- **Close-CTA variants confirmed (2 patterns):**
  - **Variant A (slide 55, VPC carousel):** single-bullet body + browser-mockup CTA card = "here's what you learned + here's the console".
  - **Variant B (slide 70, Resume/AI):** 3-bullet takeaway-recap + text-only brand-CTA card = "here's the arc-recap + here's the offer".
  - Rule: use A when the carousel has a **hands-on / tool-oriented** subject (AWS console, code, product). Use B when the carousel has a **narrative / opinion-oriented** subject (career advice, hiring trends, industry commentary).
- **Bullets use yellow dots**, not mint (mint was reserved for safe/positive/CTA in earlier slides). This is a **new role for yellow**: recap-bullet-marker. Yellow's fifth role confirmed (headline-inline-pill, stat callout, active-bar, anomaly-figure, recap-bullet).
- **The globe/lattice icon** — this is the NextWork brand mark. In our system we should include it as a static SVG asset (not procedurally generated) — but the primitive `makeNextworkGlobeIcon` handles a Fabric.js re-creation for programmatic generation. **Recommend:** ship it as `nextwork_brandmark.svg` in `backend/assets/brand/nextwork/` and load via `fabric.loadSVGFromURL` when this template is invoked.

  - **Primitive signature:** `makeLabelledFolderCardRow({ items: Array<{ levelLabel, name, folderColor }>, folderSize, gap, x, y, width, pillColor, pillTextColor, labelColor })` — highly reusable for **any listicle cover** where the levels/steps/parts are named up-front. Alternative folder-shape variants: `iconShape: "folder" | "book" | "card" | "chevron"` for different topical fits.

Followed by `NextWork` text-only wordmark. **Confirms carousel-length variant #4: 8 slides** — nextwork now observed with 3, 8, 9, and 11-slide carousels.

**Layering (back → front):**
`[dark canvas frame]` → `[inset card with painterly sky+grass artwork]` → `[6 scattered tool icons]` → `[kicker line]` → `[serif display headline: System / Design]` → `[right chevron]` → `[7 labelled folder cards with Lvl pills]` → `[dot indicator + NextWork wordmark]`.

**Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer) — listicle cover with labelled preview:**
- **Kicker formula:** `"The N [something] of"` — 3-4 words, definite article + number + category noun (`The 7 levels of`, `The 5 stages of`, `The 4 patterns for`). Signals ordered ladder.
- **Serif display headline:** 1-2 words, big concept-noun (`System Design`, `Machine Learning`, `Software Architecture`). **Fraunces Bold on concept-covers, Inter Bold on how-to-covers.**
- **Level names:** 1-word or 2-word noun (`Basics`, `Scaling`, `Caching`, `Data`, `Async`, `Resilience`, `Tradeoffs`). All same casing (Title Case). Progressively technical top-to-bottom. Reader should be able to gauge the arc from names alone.
- **Level count:** 5–7 works well; 4 feels short, 8+ makes cards too small on the folder-row primitive.
- **Colour sequence for the folder-row:** always use a **sequential-heat palette** (blue → green → yellow → orange → red → black) for progression carousels. For *unordered* categorisation carousels, use a **categorical palette** (distinct hues, not sequential). Recommend the LLM writer emit `carousel.progressionType: "ordered" | "categorical"` to trigger the correct palette.

**Notes:**
- **Brand-family expansion confirmed.** Nextwork now has 4 mapped carousel-mood combinations:
  - **Warm-vineyard / VPC:** how-to procedural, Inter Bold headlines, sunset painterly.
  - **Cool-forest-fog / Security-skills:** career/data-driven, Inter Bold + scattered icons.
  - **Dusk-hill / Resume-AI:** commentary/opinion, Inter Bold + rich body evidence.
  - **Bright-sky-hill / System-Design (NEW):** foundational/conceptual, Fraunces Bold serif + labelled preview.
- **The kicker + serif + folder-row combo is a *sub-template* of `aurora-nextwork-cover`.** Recommend the template registry distinguish `aurora-nextwork-cover-scatter` (slide 48/57/64 pattern) from `aurora-nextwork-cover-listicle-preview` (this slide 71 pattern). The two are cousins, not identical.
- **First observation of a colour-narrative palette on nextwork.** The folder-row's blue→black progression is a *communication device* separate from the standard component-color contract seen in the VPC carousel. Two different palette systems exist: (a) **component-locked** (subnet=blue, IGW=mint, ACL=cream — used to signal semantic category), (b) **sequential-progression** (light→dark = easy→hard — used to signal ordinal ranking). LLM writer must know which system applies before selecting colours.
- **Prediction for slide 72 (image copy 24, my next read):** Level 1 (Basics) body slide. Almost certainly follows the same skill-deep-dive layout as slides 59–61 (from the Security-Skills carousel), but with a **level-pill + folder-card mini-header** instead of a salary-bar chart — swapping the "salary-comparison" hero-embed for a "level-mechanics" diagram or code snippet. Watch for whether the folder colour from slide 71's preview row (blue for `Basics`) is honoured on the body slide's accent — this would confirm carousel-locked colour contract holds even across NEW palette systems.

**Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer) — level-deep-dive body slide:**
- **Header structure:** folder-card icon (colour-locked from cover) → level pill (`Lvl N`) → single-word title (`Basics`, `Scaling`, `Caching`).
- **Body-paragraph formula:** 2-sentence, ~25-30 words. Structure: `[definition or metaphor sentence]. [Forward-reference to how this concept scales through the rest of the carousel].` Voice: aphoristic, plainspoken, present tense. **The forward-reference is the signature move** — grounds this concept in the carousel's ladder metaphor.
- **Hero diagram:** ONE core diagram illustrating the concept. Cream card, minimal labels, always includes a small italic caption below (`every big system is this loop, many times over`). Caption is a **generalization** of the diagram — makes the specific visual feel universal.
- **Use-case chip row formula:** label `use it for`, then 2 chips. Each chip is a **short use-case scenario** (3-6 words, gerund verb-phrase): `sketching a new service`, `explaining a system in an interview`, `debugging a slow endpoint`, `estimating server capacity`. Chips are always concrete reader-facing tasks, not abstract skills.
- **Voice rule:** the caption + chip labels are **all lowercase** (except proper nouns) — signals conversational, notebook-margin voice. Contrasts the Title Case level names.

- **Diagram caption formula:** 1-sentence, 10-15 words, present tense, ends with an outcome (`no single machine ever drowns`, `you never have to open the filing cabinet twice`). The caption is a **plainspoken restatement of the diagram's teaching**.

Reusable for: databases (Postgres / MongoDB / DynamoDB), queues (SQS / Kafka / RabbitMQ), monitoring (Datadog / New Relic / Grafana), CDNs (Cloudflare / Fastly / Akamai), etc.
  - **Reusability:** 5/5 — universal for tool-taxonomy body slides.

- **Dot indicator** (y≈760, x-centred): 11 dots, dot 4 filled.

**NEW primitives / tokens (flag for build):**
- `makeToolCardColumn` (as above) — vertical stack of 3 tool cards.
- `makeToolCard({ brandLogo, name, capability, cardFillColor, cardRadius, width, height, x, y })` — single tool-card sub-primitive.
- **`NEXTWORK_TOOL_CARD_CREAM = #F5F0E4`** — slightly lighter cream than `NEXTWORK_CREAM_NEUTRAL` (#F0EAD8). Distinct fill for tool cards vs diagram cards, creating a subtle 2-tier cream hierarchy: **diagram card = darker cream (background surface)**, **tool card = lighter cream (foreground callouts)**. Recommend documenting the difference for LLM writer.
- Extension: `makeUseCaseChipRow` gains `orientation: "horizontal" | "vertical"` and `chip.count: 2 | 3` variants.

**Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer) — level-deep-dive layout-C (tool-taxonomy):**
- **Use layout-C when the level concept has multiple layered real-world tool implementations.** Best for topics where "here's the concept" is enriched by "here are 3 concrete products you'd actually use". Examples:
  - `Caching` — Redis (memory) / Cloudflare (edge) / Nginx (proxy).
  - `Queueing` — SQS (managed) / Kafka (log) / RabbitMQ (broker).
  - `Monitoring` — Datadog (SaaS) / Prometheus (self-hosted) / New Relic (APM).
- **Body-paragraph formula for layout-C — `proverb-with-example`:** open with an aphorism (`The fastest request is the one you never make twice`), then name a real-world implementation (`Instagram caches feed data…`). The proverb is quotable/memorable; the example makes it concrete.
- **Tool-card contract:** `brandLogo` must be a real product logo (fair-use editorial reference, same brand-safety guardrail as slide 66's Rezi.ai / slide 69's Google-Cisco-McKinsey). `capability` is a 3-6 word gerund/verb phrase describing WHAT the tool does at that layer (`caches query results in memory`, `caches files at the edge`, `caches responses for your app`). All lowercase — conversational voice.
- **Rule:** exactly 3 tool cards. 2 = feels incomplete, 4+ = card sizes shrink below readability.
- **Chip-row variant:** 3-chip vertical stacks work well when the left column has extra vertical space (as here). Use `orientation: "horizontal"` when chip labels are short (`handling a traffic spike`) and space is tight; `orientation: "vertical"` when labels are punchy 3-word phrases (`serve repeat reads fast`, `protect the database`, `handle traffic spikes`).

**Notes:**
- **THREE bodyLayout variants confirmed within a single carousel:**
  - **Variant A (bottom-diagram):** slide 72 Basics.
  - **Variant B (top-diagram):** slide 73 Scaling.
  - **Variant C (tool-column):** this slide, Caching.
  - Confirms nextwork designers choose layout **per-slide based on the concept's teaching needs**, not a carousel-wide default. LLM writer should support all 3 as first-class options and pick based on: `does this concept have 3+ real-world tool implementations? → C`, `is the diagram visually dramatic? → B`, `does the concept need thesis-first framing? → A`.
- **Instagram is the named example** — nextwork calls out real companies as case studies within the body paragraph. Confirms editorial voice can reference **any major tech brand** as an example of the concept in action. Recommend our LLM writer support `paragraph.namedCompanyExample: string` slot.
- **The proverb-opener (`The fastest request is the one you never make twice`)** is one of the most **quotable lines** in the entire nextwork corpus. Signals nextwork occasionally lifts a body paragraph into aphorism territory — creating **shareable / screenshot-worthy** moments within the carousel. Recommend our LLM writer target **one aphoristic line per carousel** as a "quotable moment" strategy.

**Copy-pattern hint (LLM writer) — cover template:**
- Overline formula: `[* asterisk] the [N] [stages | levels | steps | rules] [of | to | for] [gerund + goal-noun]`. Examples: `the 7 levels of System Design`, `the 7 stages of becoming a Platform Engineer`, `the 5 steps to launch a startup`, `the 3 rules of scaling teams`.
- Title formula: `[topic phrase, 1-3 words per line, unbounded serif] + [yellow-highlight-box on final "topic anchor" or clarifier word]`. The highlight-box word is often a **role, deliverable, or descriptor** (`System Design`, `Roadmap`, `Playbook`, `Guide`, `Cheatsheet`).
- Hero-content formula: emit **one visualization** that previews the carousel's shape (folder-preview-row = "here are the levels", bar-chart = "here's the progression / stakes", timeline = "here's the path"). The visualization **should encode a progression** (colour ramp, size ramp, or position ramp) matching the carousel's underlying ordinal structure.
- Sourcing formula (when data is shown): `[metric] [population/scope] · [source] [year]` — single line, low visual weight, Inter Bold small.

Palette predictions for those levels (rust-red `#C85040` and near-black `#141414`) are **unverified** and should be flagged as **inferred, not observed** in the global synthesis.
- **Cover-template reuse across carousels** is a huge win: nextwork's designers built a strong template once (slide 71) and reused the shell for a new topic (slide 77) with just a different hero-visualization. LLM writer + Fabric renderer should treat `aurora-nextwork-cover` as a **first-class reusable cover template**, parameterized on:
  - Overline text
  - Title text (line-break-aware)
  - Highlight-box word (last word gets the box)
  - Hero-content descriptor (`type` + `data`)
  - Dot count (= slide count of this carousel)
- **The mustard-yellow bar colour (`#D4A845`)** is deeper than the highlight-box's butter-yellow (`#F0E098`). Deliberate: highlight-box needs to feel **soft/friendly** (typographic emphasis), bars need to feel **substantive** (data). Same hue family, different value/saturation to serve different communication roles. **LLM writer / theme system: recommend `PALETTE_YELLOW_SOFT` (highlight-box) and `PALETTE_YELLOW_STRONG` (data/emphasis) as separate tokens** rather than a single yellow.
- **The bar chart tells a story** (`entry → typical → senior = $128k → $168k → $205k`, a 60% climb across the journey). This is a **teaser for what the carousel promises** — the reader is hooked with a concrete number-reward. Recommend LLM writer look for **hero-visualizations that encode the payoff of the carousel** (numeric outcome, trajectory, endpoint), not just decorative.

Given the carousel-cover reuse pattern, expect **body-slide template reuse** too: same folder-header primitive + stage-pill + title-underline-rule. Recommend LLM writer default to **concrete-example primitives** (terminal, browser, console) over abstract descriptions when the topic has a canonical UI/CLI representation.

The terminal mockup content **reinforces the paragraph's argument**. Recommend LLM writer coordinate `terminal.lines` content with the paragraph's thesis — the terminal should be an *example* of what the paragraph *argues for*.
